The grant shall be in an amount for the direct costs of the services or supports outlined in the service agreement.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) Reimbursable costs shall not include costs for resources already available, such as special education classes, day training and habilitation, case management, other services to which the person is entitled, medical costs covered by insurance or other health programs, or other resources usually available at no cost to the person or the person's legal representative or other authorized representative.</p>
          <p class="in">(h) The state of Minnesota, the county boards participating in the consumer support grant program, or the agencies acting on behalf of the county boards in the implementation and administration of the consumer support grant program shall not be liable for damages, injuries, or liabilities sustained through the purchase of support by the individual, the individual's family, or the authorized representative under this section with funds received through the consumer support grant program. Liabilities include but are not limited to: workers' compensation liability, the Federal Insurance Contributions Act (FICA), or the Federal Unemployment Tax Act (FUTA). For purposes of this section, participating county boards and agencies acting on behalf of county boards are exempt from the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/268.035">268.035</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.476.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Reimbursement, allocations, and reporting.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) For the purpose of transferring persons to the consumer support grant program from the family support program and personal care assistance services, home health aide services, or home care nursing services, the amount of funds transferred by the commissioner between the family support program account, the medical assistance account, or the consumer support grant account shall be based on each county's participation in transferring persons to the consumer support grant program from those programs and services.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) At the beginning of each fiscal year, county allocations for consumer support grants shall be based on:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the number of persons to whom the county board expects to provide consumer supports grants;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) their eligibility for current program and services;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the monthly grant levels allowed under subdivision 11; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) projected dates when persons will start receiving grants. County allocations shall be adjusted periodically by the commissioner based on the actual transfer of persons or service openings, and the monthly grant levels associated with those persons or service openings, to the consumer support grant program.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The amount of funds transferred by the commissioner from the medical assistance account for an individual may be changed if it is determined by the county or its agent that the individual's need for support has changed.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The authority to utilize funds transferred to the consumer support grant account for the purposes of implementing and administering the consumer support grant program will not be limited or constrained by the spending authority provided to the program of origination.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) The commissioner may use up to five percent of each county's allocation, as adjusted, for payments for administrative expenses, to be paid as a proportionate addition to reported direct service expenditures.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) The county allocation for each person or the person's legal representative or other authorized representative cannot exceed the amount allowed under subdivision 11.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) The commissioner may recover, suspend, or withhold payments if the county board, local agency, or grantee does not comply with the requirements of this section.</p>
          <p class="in">(h) Grant funds unexpended by consumers shall return to the state once a year. The annual return of unexpended grant funds shall occur in the quarter following the end of the state fiscal year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.476.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Right to appeal.</span></h2>
          <p>Notice, appeal, and hearing procedures shall be conducted in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.045">256.045</a>. The denial, suspension, or termination of services under this program may be appealed by a recipient or applicant under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.045#stat.256.045.3">256.045, subdivision 3</a>. It is an absolute defense to an appeal under this section, if the county board proves that it followed the established written procedures and criteria and determined that the grant could not be provided within the county board's allocation of money for consumer support grants.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.476.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/9/#laws.3.76.0">1Sp2001 c 9 art 3 s 76</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.476.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Commissioner responsibilities.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall:</p>
          <p>(1) transfer and allocate funds pursuant to subdivision 11;</p>
          <p>(2) determine allocations based on projected and actual local agency use;</p>
          <p>(3) monitor and oversee overall program spending;</p>
          <p>(4) evaluate the effectiveness of the program;</p>
          <p>(5) provide training and technical assistance for local agencies and consumers to help identify potential applicants to the program; and</p>
          <p>(6) develop guidelines for local agency program administration and consumer information.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.476.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">County board responsibilities.</span></h2>
          <p>County boards receiving funds under this section shall:</p>
          <p>(1) determine the needs of persons and families for services and supports;</p>
          <p>(2) determine the eligibility for persons proposed for program participation;</p>
          <p>(3) approve items and services to be reimbursed and inform families of their determination;</p>
          <p>(4) issue support grants directly to or on behalf of persons;</p>
          <p>(5) submit quarterly financial reports and an annual program report to the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(6) coordinate services and supports with other programs offered or made available to persons or their families; and</p>
          <p>(7) provide assistance to persons or their families in securing or maintaining supports, as needed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.476.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Consumer responsibilities.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Persons receiving grants under this section shall:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) spend the grant money in a manner consistent with their agreement with the local agency;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) notify the local agency of any necessary changes in the grant or the items on which it is spent;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) notify the local agency of any decision made by the person, a person's legal representative, or other authorized representative that would change their eligibility for consumer support grants;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) arrange and pay for supports; and</p>
          <p class="in">(5) inform the local agency of areas where they have experienced difficulty securing or maintaining supports.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.476.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Consumer support grant program after July 1, 2001.</span></h2>
          <p class="in"> Effective July 1, 2001, the commissioner shall allocate consumer support grant resources to serve additional individuals based on a review of Medicaid authorization and payment information of persons eligible for a consumer support grant from the most recent fiscal year. The commissioner shall use the following methodology to calculate maximum allowable monthly consumer support grant levels:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) For individuals whose program of origination is medical assistance home care under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0651">256B.0651</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0653">256B.0653</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0654">256B.0654</a>, the maximum allowable monthly grant levels are calculated by:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) determining the service authorization for each individual based on the individual's home care assessment;</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) calculating the overall ratio of actual payments to service authorizations by program;</p>
          <p class="in">(iii) applying the overall ratio to 50 percent of the service authorization level of each home care rating; and</p>
          <p class="in">(iv) adjusting the result for any authorized rate changes provided by the legislature.</p>
          <p>(2) The monthly consumer support grant level for individuals who are eligible for ten or more hours of personal care assistance services or community first services and supports per day shall be increased by 7.5 percent of the monthly grant amount calculated under clause (1) when the individual uses direct support services provided by a worker who has completed training as identified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0659#stat.256B.0659.11">256B.0659, subdivision 11</a>, paragraph (d), or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.85#stat.256B.85.16">256B.85, subdivision 16</a>, paragraph (e).</p>
          <p class="in">(3) The commissioner shall ensure the methodology is consistent with the home care programs.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.476.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/207/#laws.3.15.0">1995 c 207 art 3 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/203/#laws.4.12.0">1997 c 203 art 4 s 12</a>-15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/10/#laws.0.1.0">1999 c 10 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/249/#laws.0.2.0">1999 c 249 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/9/#laws.3.9.0">1Sp2001 c 9 art 3 s 9</a>-15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.1.113.0">2002 c 379 art 1 s 113</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.3.11.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 3 s 11</a>-15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.6.3.0">2007 c 147 art 6 s 3</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/277/#laws.1.33.0">2008 c 277 art 1 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/286/#laws.1.3.0">2008 c 286 art 1 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/79/#laws.8.14.0">2009 c 79 art 8 s 14</a>,15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.9.9.0">2012 c 216 art 9 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.4.9.0">2014 c 262 art 4 s 9; art 5 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/291/#laws.9.5.0">2014 c 291 art 9 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/7/#laws.13.6.0">1Sp2021 c 7 art 13 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.4764">
        <h1 class="shn">256.4764 LONG-TERM SERVICES AND SUPPORTS WORKFORCE INCENTIVE GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4764.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Grant program established.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services shall establish grants for long-term services and supports providers and facilities to assist with recruiting and retaining direct support professionals.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4764.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Commissioner" means the commissioner of human services.</p>
          <p>(c) "Eligible employer" means an organization enrolled in a Minnesota health care program that is:</p>
          <p>(1) a provider of home and community-based services under chapter 245D;</p>
          <p>(2) a facility certified as an intermediate care facility for persons with developmental disabilities;</p>
          <p>(3) a nursing facility under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256R.02#stat.256R.02.33">256R.02, subdivision 33</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) a provider of personal care assistance services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0659">256B.0659</a>;</p>
          <p>(5) a provider of community first services and supports under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.85">256B.85</a>;</p>
          <p>(6) a provider of early intensive developmental and behavioral intervention services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0949">256B.0949</a>;</p>
          <p>(7) a provider of home care services as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0651">256B.0651, subdivision </a>1, paragraph (d);</p>
          <p>(8) an eligible financial management services provider serving people through consumer-directed community supports under chapter 256S and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.092">256B.092</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.49">256B.49</a>, or consumer support grants under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.476">256.476</a>; or</p>
          <p>(9) a provider of customized living services as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256S.02">256S.02</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) "Eligible worker" means a worker who earns $30 per hour or less and is currently employed or recruited to be employed by an eligible employer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4764.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Allowable uses of grant money.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Grantees must use grant money to provide payments to eligible workers for the following purposes:</p>
          <p>(1) retention, recruitment, and incentive payments;</p>
          <p>(2) postsecondary loan and tuition payments;</p>
          <p>(3) child care costs;</p>
          <p>(4) transportation-related costs;</p>
          <p>(5) personal care assistant background study costs; and</p>
          <p>(6) other costs associated with retaining and recruiting workers, as approved by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) Eligible workers may receive cumulative payments up to $1,000 per calendar year from the workforce incentive grant account and all other state money intended for the same purpose. Workers are not eligible for payments under this section if they received payments under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.4766">256.4766</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner must develop a grant cycle distribution plan that allows for equitable distribution of money among eligible employers. The commissioner's determination of the grant awards and amounts is final and is not subject to appeal.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4764.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Attestation.</span></h2>
          <p>As a condition of obtaining grant payments under this section, an eligible employer must attest and agree to the following:</p>
          <p>(1) the employer is an eligible employer;</p>
          <p>(2) the total number of eligible employees;</p>
          <p>(3) the employer will distribute the entire value of the grant to eligible workers allowed under this section;</p>
          <p>(4) the employer will create and maintain records under subdivision 6;</p>
          <p>(5) the employer will not use the money appropriated under this section for any purpose other than the purposes permitted under this section; and</p>
          <p>(6) the entire value of any grant amounts will be distributed to eligible workers identified by the employer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4764.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Distribution plan; report.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Each grantee shall prepare, and upon request submit to the commissioner, a distribution plan that specifies the amount of money the grantee expects to receive and how that money will be distributed for recruitment and retention purposes for eligible employees. Within 60 days of receiving the grant, the grantee must post the distribution plan and leave it posted for a period of at least six months in an area of the grantee's operation to which all direct support professionals have access.</p>
          <p>(b) Within 12 months of receiving a grant under this section, each grantee that receives a grant shall submit a report to the commissioner that includes the following information:</p>
          <p>(1) a description of how grant money was distributed to eligible employees; and</p>
          <p>(2) the total dollar amount distributed.</p>
          <p>(c) Failure to submit the report under paragraph (b) may result in recoupment of grant money.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4764.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Audits and recoupment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may perform an audit under this section up to six years after a grant is awarded to ensure:</p>
          <p>(1) the grantee used the money solely for allowable purposes under subdivision 3;</p>
          <p>(2) the grantee was truthful when making attestations under subdivision 4; and</p>
          <p>(3) the grantee complied with the conditions of receiving a grant under this section.</p>
          <p>(b) If the commissioner determines that a grantee used grant money for purposes not authorized under this section, the commissioner must treat any amount used for a purpose not authorized under this section as an overpayment. The commissioner must recover any overpayment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4764.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Grants not to be considered income.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, grant awards under this section must not be considered income, assets, or personal property for purposes of determining eligibility or recertifying eligibility for:</p>
          <p>(1) child care assistance programs under chapter 142E;</p>
          <p>(2) general assistance, Minnesota supplemental aid, and food support under chapter 256D;</p>
          <p>(3) housing support under chapter 256I;</p>
          <p>(4) the Minnesota family investment program and diversionary work program under chapter 142G; and</p>
          <p>(5) economic assistance programs under chapter 256P.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner must not consider grant awards under this section as income or assets under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.056#stat.256B.056.1a">256B.056, subdivision 1a</a>, paragraph (a), 3, or 3c, or for persons with eligibility determined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.057#stat.256B.057.3">256B.057, subdivision 3</a>, 3a, 3b, 4, or 9.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4764.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Income tax subtractions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, "subtraction" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.0132#stat.290.0132.1">290.0132, subdivision 1</a>, and the rules in that subdivision apply for this section. The definitions in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.01">290.01</a> apply to this section.</p>
          <p>(b) The amount of a payment received under this section is a subtraction.</p>
          <p>(c) Payments under this section and Laws 2021, First Special Session chapter 7, article 17, section 20, as amended, are excluded from income as defined in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.0674">290.0674</a>, subdivision 2a, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290A.03#stat.290A.03.3">290A.03, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4764.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Account created.</span></h2>
          <p>A workforce incentive grant account is created in the special revenue fund. Appropriations made for grants and payments administered under this section may be transferred to this account. Amounts in the account are appropriated to the commissioner of human services. Appropriations transferred to this account cancel and are returned to the fund of origin on the date the original appropriations would have lapsed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4764.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Nursing facilities; applicable credit.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must treat grant payments awarded under this section as an applicable credit as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256R.10">256R.10</a>, subdivision 6.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4764.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Self-directed services workforce.</span></h2>
          <p>Payments administered under this section, including reimbursements for paid family medical leave premiums, do not constitute a change in a term or condition for individual providers as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0711">256B.0711</a> in covered programs and are not subject to the state's obligation to meet and negotiate under chapter 179A.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.4764.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/61/#laws.1.10.0">2023 c 61 art 1 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.4.26.0">2024 c 80 art 4 s 26</a>; art 5 s 7; art 7 s 12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.42.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/125/#laws.1.10.0">2024 c 125 art 1 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.46.10.0">2024 c 127 art 46 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.4766">
        <h1 class="shn">256.4766 NURSING FACILITY WORKFORCE INCENTIVE GRANT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4766.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Grant program established.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services shall establish grants for nursing facilities to assist with recruiting and retaining eligible workers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4766.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Commissioner" means the commissioner of human services.</p>
          <p>(c) "Eligible employer" means a nursing facility as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256R.02">256R.02</a>, subdivision 33.</p>
          <p>(d) "Eligible worker" means a worker who earns $30 per hour or less and is currently employed or recruited to be employed by an eligible employer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4766.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Allowable uses of grant money.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Grantees must use grant money to provide payments to eligible workers for the following purposes:</p>
          <p>(1) retention, recruitment, and incentive payments;</p>
          <p>(2) employee-owned benefits, such as health savings accounts, HRSA, and flexible spending accounts;</p>
          <p>(3) employee contributions to a 401k account;</p>
          <p>(4) education, professional development, and financial counseling;</p>
          <p>(5) child care, meals, transportation, and housing;</p>
          <p>(6) health and wellness; and</p>
          <p>(7) other flexible needs related to workforce challenges as determined by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) An eligible worker may receive payments of up to $3,000 per year from the workforce incentive grant account and all other state money intended for the same purpose.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner must develop a grant cycle distribution plan that allows for equitable distribution of money among eligible employers. The commissioner's determination of the grant awards and amounts is final and is not subject to appeal.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4766.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Attestation.</span></h2>
          <p>As a condition of obtaining grant payments under this section, an eligible employer must attest and agree to the following:</p>
          <p>(1) the employer is an eligible employer;</p>
          <p>(2) the total number of eligible employees;</p>
          <p>(3) the employer will distribute the entire value of the grant to eligible workers allowed under this section;</p>
          <p>(4) the employer will create and maintain records under subdivision 6;</p>
          <p>(5) the employer will not use the money appropriated under this section for any purpose other than the purposes permitted under this section; and</p>
          <p>(6) the entire value of any grant amounts will be distributed to eligible workers identified by the employer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4766.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Distribution plan; report.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Each grantee shall prepare, and upon request submit to the commissioner, a distribution plan that specifies the amount of money the grantee expects to receive and how that money will be distributed for workforce incentives for eligible employees. Within 60 days of receiving the grant, the grantee must post the distribution plan and leave the plan posted for a period of at least six months in an area of the grantee's operation to which all direct support professionals have access.</p>
          <p>(b) Within 12 months of receiving a grant under this section, each grantee that receives a grant shall submit a report to the commissioner that includes the following information:</p>
          <p>(1) a description of how grant money was distributed to eligible employees; and</p>
          <p>(2) the total dollar amount distributed.</p>
          <p>(c) Failure to submit the report under paragraph (b) will result in recoupment of grant money.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4766.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Audits and recoupment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may perform an audit under this section up to six years after a grant is awarded to ensure that:</p>
          <p>(1) the grantee used the money solely for allowable purposes under subdivision 3;</p>
          <p>(2) the grantee was truthful when making attestations under subdivision 4; and</p>
          <p>(3) the grantee complied with the conditions of receiving a grant under this section.</p>
          <p>(b) If the commissioner determines that a grantee used grant money for purposes not authorized under this section, the commissioner must treat any amount used for a purpose not authorized under this section as an overpayment. The commissioner must recover any overpayment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4766.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Grants not to be considered income.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, grant awards under this section must not be considered income, assets, or personal property for purposes of determining eligibility or recertifying eligibility for:</p>
          <p>(1) child care assistance programs under chapter 142E;</p>
          <p>(2) general assistance, Minnesota supplemental aid, and food support under chapter 256D;</p>
          <p>(3) housing support under chapter 256I;</p>
          <p>(4) the Minnesota family investment program and diversionary work program under chapter 142G; and</p>
          <p>(5) economic assistance programs under chapter 256P.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner must not consider grant awards under this section as income or assets under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.056#stat.256B.056.1a">256B.056, subdivision 1a</a>, paragraph (a), 3, or 3c, or for persons with eligibility determined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.057#stat.256B.057.3">256B.057, subdivision 3</a>, 3a, 3b, 4, or 9.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4766.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Income tax subtractions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, "subtraction" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.0132#stat.290.0132.1">290.0132, subdivision 1</a>, and the rules in that subdivision apply for this section. The definitions in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.01">290.01</a> apply to this section.</p>
          <p>(b) The amount of a payment received under this section is a subtraction.</p>
          <p>(c) Payments under this section are excluded from income as defined in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.0674">290.0674</a>, subdivision 2a, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290A.03#stat.290A.03.3">290A.03, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4766.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Account created.</span></h2>
          <p>A nursing facility workforce incentive grant account is created in the special revenue fund. Appropriations made for grants and payments administered under this section may be transferred to this account. Amounts in the account are appropriated to the commissioner of human services. Appropriations transferred to this account cancel and are returned to the fund of origin on the date the original appropriations would have lapsed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4766.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Nursing facilities; applicable credit.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must treat grant payments awarded under this section as an applicable credit as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256R.10">256R.10</a>, subdivision 6.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.4766.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/74/#laws.0.1.0">2023 c 74 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.4.26.0">2024 c 80 art 4 s 26</a>; art 5 s 7; art 7 s 12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.42.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 42</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.477">
        <h1 class="shn">256.477 SELF-ADVOCACY GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.477.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">The Rick Cardenas Statewide Self-Advocacy Network.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall make available a grant for the purposes of establishing and maintaining the Rick Cardenas Statewide Self-Advocacy Network for persons with intellectual and developmental disabilities. The Rick Cardenas Statewide Self-Advocacy Network shall:</p>
          <p>(1) ensure that persons with intellectual and developmental disabilities are informed of their rights in employment, housing, transportation, voting, government policy, and other issues pertinent to the intellectual and developmental disability community;</p>
          <p>(2) provide public education and awareness of the civil and human rights issues persons with intellectual and developmental disabilities face;</p>
          <p>(3) provide funds, technical assistance, and other resources for self-advocacy groups across the state;</p>
          <p>(4) organize systems of communications to facilitate an exchange of information between self-advocacy groups;</p>
          <p>(5) train and support the activities of a statewide network of peer-to-peer mentors for persons with developmental disabilities focused on building awareness among people with developmental disabilities of service options; assisting people with developmental disabilities choose service options; and developing the advocacy skills of people with developmental disabilities necessary for them to move toward full inclusion in community life, including by developing and delivering a curriculum to support the peer-to-peer network;</p>
          <p>(6) provide outreach activities, including statewide conferences and disability networking opportunities, focused on self-advocacy, informed choice, and community engagement skills; and</p>
          <p>(7) provide an annual leadership program for persons with intellectual and developmental disabilities.</p>
          <p>(b) An organization receiving a grant under paragraph (a) must be an organization governed by people with intellectual and developmental disabilities that administers a statewide network of disability groups in order to maintain and promote self-advocacy services and supports for persons with intellectual and developmental disabilities throughout the state.</p>
          <p>(c) An organization receiving a grant under this subdivision may use a portion of grant revenue determined by the commissioner for administration and general operating costs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.477.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Subgrants for outreach to persons in institutional settings.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall make available to an organization described under subdivision 1 a grant for subgrants to organizations in Minnesota to conduct outreach to persons working and living in institutional settings to provide education and information about community options. Subgrant funds must be used to deliver peer-led skill training sessions in six regions of the state to help persons with intellectual and developmental disabilities understand community service options related to:</p>
          <p>(1) housing;</p>
          <p>(2) employment;</p>
          <p>(3) education;</p>
          <p>(4) transportation;</p>
          <p>(5) emerging service reform initiatives contained in the state's Olmstead plan; the Workforce Innovation and Opportunity Act, Public Law 113-128; and federal home and community-based services regulations; and</p>
          <p>(6) connecting with individuals who can help persons with intellectual and developmental disabilities make an informed choice and plan for a transition in services.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.477.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.1.4.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 1 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/7/#laws.13.7.0">1Sp2021 c 7 art 13 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.4772">
        <h1 class="shn">256.4772 MINNESOTA INCLUSION INITIATIVE GRANT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4772.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Grant program established.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services shall establish the Minnesota inclusion initiative grant program to encourage self-advocacy groups of persons with intellectual and developmental disabilities to develop and organize projects that increase the inclusion of persons with intellectual and developmental disabilities in the community, improve community integration outcomes, educate decision-makers and the public about persons with intellectual and developmental disabilities, including the systemic barriers that prevent them from being included in the community, and to advocate for changes that increase access to formal and informal supports and services necessary for greater inclusion of persons with intellectual and developmental disabilities in the community.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4772.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Administration.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services, as authorized by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.01#stat.256.01.2">256.01, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (a), clause (6), shall issue a request for proposals to contract with a public or private entity to (1) serve as a fiscal host for the money appropriated for the purposes described in this section, and (2) develop guidelines, criteria, and procedures for awarding grants. The fiscal host shall establish an advisory committee consisting of self-advocates, nonprofit advocacy organizations, and Department of Human Services staff to review applications and award grants under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4772.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Applications.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Entities seeking grants under this section shall apply to the advisory committee of the fiscal host under contract with the commissioner. The grant applicant must include a description of the project that the applicant is proposing, the amount of money that the applicant is seeking, and a proposed budget describing how the applicant will spend the grant money.</p>
          <p>(b) The advisory committee may award grants to applicants only for projects that meet the requirements of subdivision 4.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4772.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Use of grant money.</span></h2>
          <p>Projects funded by grant money must have person-centered goals, call attention to issues that limit inclusion of persons with intellectual and developmental disabilities, address barriers to inclusion that persons with intellectual and developmental disabilities face in their communities, or increase the inclusion of persons with intellectual and developmental disabilities in their communities. Applicants may propose strategies to increase inclusion of persons with intellectual and developmental disabilities in their communities by:</p>
          <p>(1) decreasing barriers to workforce participation experienced by persons with intellectual and developmental disabilities;</p>
          <p>(2) overcoming barriers to accessible and reliable transportation options for persons with intellectual and developmental disabilities;</p>
          <p>(3) identifying and addressing barriers to voting experienced by persons with intellectual and developmental disabilities;</p>
          <p>(4) advocating for increased accessible housing for persons with intellectual and developmental disabilities;</p>
          <p>(5) working with governmental agencies or businesses on accessibility issues under the Americans with Disabilities Act;</p>
          <p>(6) increasing collaboration between self-advocacy groups and other organizations to effectively address systemic issues that impact persons with intellectual and developmental disabilities;</p>
          <p>(7) increasing capacity for inclusion in a community; or</p>
          <p>(8) providing public education and awareness of the civil and human rights of persons with intellectual and developmental disabilities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4772.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Reports.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Grant recipients shall provide the advisory committee with a report about the activities funded by the grant program in a format and at a time specified by the advisory committee. The advisory committee shall require grant recipients to include in the grant recipient's report at least the information necessary for the advisory committee to meet the advisory committee's obligation under paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(b) The advisory committee shall provide the commissioner with a report that describes all of the activities and outcomes of projects funded by the grant program in a format and at a time determined by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.4772.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/7/#laws.13.8.0">1Sp2021 c 7 art 13 s 8</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.4773">
        <h1 class="shn">256.4773 TECHNOLOGY FOR HOME GRANT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4773.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must establish a technology for home grant program that provides assistive technology consultations and resources for people with disabilities who want to stay in their own home, move to their own home, or remain in a less restrictive residential setting. The grant program may be administered using a team approach that allows multiple professionals to assess and meet a person's assistive technology needs. The team may include but is not limited to occupational therapists, physical therapists, speech therapists, nurses, and engineers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4773.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligible applicants.</span></h2>
          <p>An eligible applicant is a person who uses or is eligible for home care services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0651">256B.0651</a>, home and community-based services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.092">256B.092</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.49">256B.49</a>, personal care assistance under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0659">256B.0659</a>, or community first services and supports under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.85">256B.85</a>, and who meets one of the following conditions:</p>
          <p>(1) lives in the applicant's own home and may benefit from assistive technology for safety, communication, community engagement, or independence;</p>
          <p>(2) is currently seeking to live in the applicant's own home and needs assistive technology to meet that goal; or</p>
          <p>(3) resides in a residential setting under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.4914#stat.256B.4914.3">256B.4914, subdivision 3</a>, and is seeking to reduce reliance on paid staff to live more independently in the setting.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4773.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Allowable grant activities.</span></h2>
          <p>The technology for home grant program must provide at-home, in-person assistive technology consultation and technical assistance to help people with disabilities live more independently. Allowable activities include but are not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) consultations in people's homes, workplaces, or community locations;</p>
          <p>(2) connecting people to resources to help them live in their own homes, transition to their own homes, or live more independently in residential settings;</p>
          <p>(3) conducting training for and set up and installation of assistive technology; and</p>
          <p>(4) participating on a person's care team to develop a plan to ensure assistive technology goals are met.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4773.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Data collection and outcomes.</span></h2>
          <p>Grantees must provide data summaries to the commissioner for the purpose of evaluating the effectiveness of the grant program. The commissioner must identify outcome measures to evaluate program activities to assess whether the grant programs help people transition to or remain in the least restrictive setting.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.4773.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/61/#laws.1.11.0">2023 c 61 art 1 s 11</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.4776">
        <h1 class="shn">256.4776 PARENT-TO-PARENT PEER SUPPORT.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner shall make a grant to an alliance member of Parent to Parent USA to support the alliance member's parent-to-parent peer support program for families of children with any type of disability or special health care needs. An eligible alliance member must have an established parent-to-parent peer support program that is statewide and represents diverse cultures and geographic locations, that conducts outreach and provides individualized support to any parent or guardian of a child with a disability or special health care need, including newly identified parents of such a child or parents experiencing transitions or changes in their child's care, and that implements best practices for peer-to-peer support, including providing support from trained parent staff and volunteer support parents who have received Parent to Parent USA's specialized parent-to-parent peer support training.</p>
        <p>(b) Grant recipients must use grant money for the purposes specified in paragraph (a).</p>
        <p>(c) For purposes of this section, "special health care needs" means disabilities, chronic illnesses or conditions, health-related educational or behavioral problems, or the risk of developing disabilities, conditions, illnesses, or problems.</p>
        <p>(d) Grant recipients must report to the commissioner of human services annually by January 15 about the services and programs funded by this grant. The report must include measurable outcomes from the previous year, including the number of families served by the organization's parent-to-parent programs and the number of volunteer support parents trained by the organization's parent-to-parent programs.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.4776.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/7/#laws.13.9.0">1Sp2021 c 7 art 13 s 9</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.478">
        <h1 class="shn">256.478 TRANSITION TO COMMUNITY INITIATIVE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.478.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose and establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall establish the transition to community initiative to award grants to serve individuals who are not eligible for medical assistance or for whom goods, supports, and services not covered by medical assistance would allow them to:</p>
          <p>(1) live in the least restrictive setting and as independently as possible;</p>
          <p>(2) access services that support short- and long-term needs for developmental growth or individualized treatment needs;</p>
          <p>(3) build or maintain relationships with family and friends; and</p>
          <p>(4) participate in community life.</p>
          <p>(b) Grantees must ensure that individuals are engaged in a process that involves person-centered planning and informed choice decision-making. The informed choice decision-making process must provide accessible written information and be experiential whenever possible, and must engage family members, legal guardians, or natural supports, as appropriate and whenever possible.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.478.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>An individual is eligible for the transition to community initiative if the individual can demonstrate that current services are not capable of meeting individual treatment and service needs that can be met in the community with support, and the individual meets at least one of the following criteria:</p>
          <p>(1) the person meets the criteria under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.092#stat.256B.092.13">256B.092, subdivision 13</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.49#stat.256B.49.24">256B.49, subdivision 24</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) the person has met treatment objectives and no longer requires a hospital-level care, residential-level care, or a secure treatment setting, but the person's discharge from the Anoka Metro Regional Treatment Center, the Minnesota Forensic Mental Health Program, the Child and Adolescent Behavioral Health Hospital program, a psychiatric residential treatment facility under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0941">256B.0941</a>, intensive residential treatment services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0622">256B.0622</a>, children's residential services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4882">245.4882</a>, juvenile detention facility, county supervised building, or a hospital would be substantially delayed without additional resources available through the transitions to community initiative;</p>
          <p>(3) the person (i) is receiving customized living services reimbursed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.4914">256B.4914</a>, 24-hour customized living services reimbursed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.4914">256B.4914</a>, or community residential services reimbursed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.4914">256B.4914</a>; (ii) expresses a desire to move; and (iii) has received approval from the commissioner; or</p>
          <p>(4) the person can demonstrate that the person's needs are beyond the scope of current service designs and grant funding can support the inclusion of additional supports for the person to access appropriate treatment and services in the least restrictive environment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.478.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Authorized uses of grant funds.</span></h2>
          <p>Grant funds may be used for but are not limited to the following:</p>
          <p>(1) increasing access to home and community-based services for an individual;</p>
          <p>(2) improving caregiver-child relationships and aiding progress toward treatment goals, including support for the individual to return to live in their home; and</p>
          <p>(3) reducing emergency department visits.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.478.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Outcomes.</span></h2>
          <p>Program evaluation is based on but not limited to the following criteria:</p>
          <p>(1) expediting discharges for individuals who no longer need hospital level of care;</p>
          <p>(2) individuals obtaining and retaining housing, including successfully returning to live with support in their home;</p>
          <p>(3) individuals maintaining community living by diverting admission to Anoka Metro Regional Treatment Center and Forensic Mental Health Program;</p>
          <p>(4) reducing recidivism rates of individuals returning to state institutions; and</p>
          <p>(5) individuals' ability to live in the least restrictive community setting.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.478.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.4.14.0">2013 c 108 art 4 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.7.25.0">2015 c 71 art 7 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/7/#laws.17.1.0">1Sp2021 c 7 art 17 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.6.22.0">2022 c 98 art 6 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.9.26.0">2023 c 70 art 9 s 26</a>,27; art 17 s 35,36</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.479">
        <h1 class="shn">256.479 CUSTOMIZED LIVING QUALITY IMPROVEMENT GRANTS.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner of human services shall develop incentive-based grants to providers of customized living services under the brain injury, community access for disability inclusion, and elderly waivers for achieving outcomes specified in a contract. The commissioner may solicit proposals from providers and implement those that, on a competitive basis, best meet the state's policy objectives.</p>
        <p>(b) To be eligible for a grant under this section, at least 75 percent of the clients served by the provider must be waiver participants. For providers of customized living services under the brain injury or community access for disability inclusion, the waiver participants must reside at multiple locations each with six or more residents. The commissioner shall give greater preference to those providers serving a higher percentage of waiver participants.</p>
        <p>(c) The commissioner shall limit expenditures under this subdivision to the amount appropriated for this purpose.</p>
        <p>(d) In establishing the specified outcomes and related criteria, the commissioner shall consider the following state policy objectives:</p>
        <p>(1) provide more efficient, higher quality services;</p>
        <p>(2) encourage home and community-based services providers to innovate;</p>
        <p>(3) equip home and community-based services providers with organizational tools and expertise to improve their quality;</p>
        <p>(4) incentivize home and community-based services providers to invest in better services; and</p>
        <p>(5) disseminate successful performance improvement strategies statewide.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.479.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.0.0.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 4 s28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/3/1/#laws.0.1.0">3Sp2020 c 1 s 1</a>,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/7/#laws.13.10.0">1Sp2021 c 7 art 13 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.4792">
        <h1 class="shn">256.4792 LONG-TERM SERVICES AND SUPPORTS LOAN PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4792.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Long-term services and supports loan program.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services shall establish a competitive loan program to provide operating loans to eligible long-term services and supports providers and facilities. The commissioner shall initiate the application process for the loan described in this section at least once annually if money is available. A second application process may be initiated each year at the discretion of the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4792.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>To be an eligible applicant for a loan under this section, a provider must submit to the commissioner of human services a loan application in the form and according to the timelines established by the commissioner. In its loan application, a loan applicant must demonstrate the following:</p>
          <p>(1) for nursing facilities with a medical assistance provider agreement that are licensed as a nursing home or boarding care home according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256R.02#stat.256R.02.33">256R.02, subdivision 33</a>:</p>
          <p>(i) the total net income of the nursing facility is not generating sufficient revenue to cover the nursing facility's operating expenses;</p>
          <p>(ii) the nursing facility is at risk of closure; and</p>
          <p>(iii) additional operating revenue is necessary to either preserve access to nursing facility services within the community or support people with complex, high-acuity support needs; and</p>
          <p>(2) for other long-term services and supports providers:</p>
          <p>(i) demonstration that the provider is enrolled in a Minnesota health care program and provides one or more of the following services in a Minnesota health care program:</p>
          <p>(A) home and community-based services under chapter 245D;</p>
          <p>(B) personal care assistance services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0659">256B.0659</a>;</p>
          <p>(C) community first services and supports under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.85">256B.85</a>;</p>
          <p>(D) early intensive developmental and behavioral intervention services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0949">256B.0949</a>;</p>
          <p>(E) home care services as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0651#stat.256B.0651.1">256B.0651, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (d); or</p>
          <p>(F) customized living services as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256S.02">256S.02</a>; and</p>
          <p>(ii) additional operating revenue is necessary to preserve access to services within the community, expand services to people within the community, expand services to new communities, or support people with complex, high-acuity support needs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4792.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Allowable uses of loan money.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A loan awarded to a nursing facility under subdivision 2, clause (1), must only be used to cover the facility's short-term operating expenses. Nursing facilities receiving loans must not use the loan proceeds to pay related organizations as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256R.02#stat.256R.02.43">256R.02, subdivision 43</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) A loan awarded to a long-term services and supports provider under subdivision 2, clause (2), must only be used to cover expenses related to achieving outcomes identified in subdivision 2, clause (2), item (ii).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4792.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Approving loans.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must evaluate all loan applications on a competitive basis and award loans to successful applicants within available appropriations for this purpose. The commissioner's decisions are final and not subject to appeal.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4792.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Disbursement schedule.</span></h2>
          <p>Successful loan applicants under this section may receive loan disbursements as a lump sum or on an agreed upon disbursement schedule. The commissioner shall approve disbursements to successful loan applicants through a memorandum of understanding. Memoranda of understanding must specify the amount and schedule of loan disbursements.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4792.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Loan administration.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may contract with an independent third party to administer the loan program under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4792.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Loan payments.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall negotiate the terms of the loan repayment, including the start of the repayment plan, the due date of the repayment, and the frequency of the repayment installments. Repayment installments must not begin until at least 18 months after the first disbursement date. The memoranda of understanding must specify the amount and schedule of loan payments. The repayment term must not exceed 72 months. If any loan payment to the commissioner is not paid within the time specified by the memoranda of understanding, the late payment must be assessed a penalty rate of 0.01 percent of the original loan amount each month the payment is past due. For nursing facilities, this late fee is not an allowable cost on the department's cost report. The commissioner shall have the power to abate penalties when discrepancies occur resulting from but not limited to circumstances of error and mail delivery.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4792.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Loan repayment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If a borrower is more than 60 calendar days delinquent in the timely payment of a contractual payment under this section, the provisions in paragraphs (b) to (e) apply.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may withhold some or all of the amount of the delinquent loan payment, together with any penalties due and owing on those amounts, from any money the department owes to the borrower. The commissioner may, at the commissioner's discretion, also withhold future contractual payments from any money the commissioner owes the provider as those contractual payments become due and owing. The commissioner may continue this withholding until the commissioner determines there is no longer any need to do so.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner shall give prior notice of the commissioner's intention to withhold by mail, facsimile, or email at least ten business days before the date of the first payment period for which the withholding begins. The notice must be deemed received as of the date of mailing or receipt of the facsimile or electronic notice. The notice must:</p>
          <p>(1) state the amount of the delinquent contractual payment;</p>
          <p>(2) state the amount of the withholding per payment period;</p>
          <p>(3) state the date on which the withholding is to begin;</p>
          <p>(4) state whether the commissioner intends to withhold future installments of the provider's contractual payments; and</p>
          <p>(5) state other contents as the commissioner deems appropriate.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner, or the commissioner's designee, may enter into written settlement agreements with a provider to resolve disputes and other matters involving unpaid loan contractual payments or future loan contractual payments.</p>
          <p>(e) Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, all unpaid loans, plus any accrued penalties, are overpayments for the purposes of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0641#stat.256B.0641.1">256B.0641, subdivision 1</a>. The current owner of a nursing home, boarding care home, or long-term services and supports provider is liable for the overpayment amount owed by a former owner for any facility sold, transferred, or reorganized.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4792.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Audit.</span></h2>
          <p>Loan money allocated under this section is subject to audit to determine whether the money was spent as authorized under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4792.8a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8a.<span class="headnote">Special revenue account.</span></h2>
          <p>A long-term services and supports loan account is created in the special revenue fund in the state treasury. Money appropriated for the purposes of this section must be transferred to the long-term services and supports loan account. All payments received under subdivision 6, along with fees, penalties, and interest, must be deposited into the special revenue account and are appropriated to the commissioner for the purposes of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4792.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Carryforward.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.28#stat.16A.28.3">16A.28, subdivision 3</a>, money in the long-term services and supports loan account for the purposes under this section carries forward and does not lapse.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4792.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.</h2>
          <p>MS 2023 Supp [Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/125/#laws.2.16.0">2024 c 125 art 2 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.47.16.0">2024 c 127 art 47 s 16</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.4792.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/61/#laws.2.12.0">2023 c 61 art 2 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/125/#laws.2.16.0">2024 c 125 art 2 s 16</a>,23; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.47.16.0">2024 c 127 art 47 s 16</a>,23</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.256.4793"><b>256.4793</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.45">142A.45</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.256.4794"><b>256.4794</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.451">142A.451</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.256.48"><b>256.48</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1947/0/535/#laws.0.16.0">1947 c 535 s 16</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">COUNCIL ON DISABILITY</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.481">
        <h1 class="shn">256.481 PERSON WITH A DISABILITY; DEFINITION.</h1>
        <p>For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.481">256.481</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.482">256.482</a> "person with a disability" means any person who:</p>
        <p>(1) has a physical, mental, or emotional impairment which substantially limits one or more major life activities;</p>
        <p>(2) has a record of such an impairment; or</p>
        <p>(3) is regarded as having such an impairment.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.481.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/757/#laws.0.1.0">1973 c 757 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/260/#laws.0.55.0">1983 c 260 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/277/#laws.0.1.0">1983 c 277 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/40/#laws.1.60.0">2017 c 40 art 1 s 60</a>,121</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.482">
        <h1 class="shn">256.482 COUNCIL ON DISABILITY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.482.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment; members.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">There is hereby established the Council on Disability which shall consist of 17 members appointed by the governor. Members shall be appointed from the general public and from organizations which provide services for persons who have a disability. A majority of council members shall be persons with a disability or parents or guardians of persons with a disability. There shall be at least one member of the council appointed from each of the state development regions. The commissioners of the Departments of Education, Human Services, Health, and Human Rights and the directors of the Rehabilitation Services and State Services for the Blind in the Department of Employment and Economic Development or their designees shall serve as ex officio members of the council without vote. In addition, the council may appoint ex officio members from other bureaus, divisions, or sections of state departments which are directly concerned with the provision of services to persons with a disability.</p>
          <p class="in">Notwithstanding the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.059">15.059</a>, each member of the council appointed by the governor shall serve a three-year term and until a successor is appointed and qualified. The compensation and removal of all members shall be as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.059">15.059</a>. The council performs functions that are not purely advisory, therefore the expiration dates provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.059">15.059</a> do not apply. The governor shall appoint a chair of the council from among the members appointed from the general public or who are persons with a disability or their parents or guardians. Vacancies shall be filled by the authority for the remainder of the unexpired term.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.482.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Executive director; staff.</span></h2>
          <p>The council may select an executive director of the council by a vote of a majority of all council members. The executive director shall be in the unclassified service of the state and shall provide administrative support for the council and provide administrative leadership to implement council mandates, policies, and objectives. The executive director shall employ and direct staff authorized according to state law and necessary to carry out council mandates, policies, activities, and objectives. The salary of the executive director and staff shall be established pursuant to chapter 43A. The executive director and staff shall be reimbursed for the actual and necessary expenses incurred as a result of their council responsibilities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.482.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Receipt of funds.</span></h2>
          <p>Whenever any person, firm, corporation, or the federal government offers to the council funds by the way of gift, grant, or loan, for purposes of assisting the council to carry out its powers and duties, the council may accept the offer by majority vote and upon acceptance the chair shall receive the funds subject to the terms of the offer. However, no money shall be accepted or received as a loan nor shall any indebtedness be incurred except in the manner and under the limitations otherwise provided by law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.482.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Organization; committees.</span></h2>
          <p>The council shall organize itself in conformity with its responsibilities under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.481">256.481</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.482">256.482</a> and shall establish committees which shall give detailed attention to the special needs of each category of persons who have a disability. The members of the committees shall be designated by the chair with the approval of a majority of the council. The council shall serve as liaison in Minnesota for the president's committee on employment of the disabled and for any other organization for which it is so designated by the governor or state legislature.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.482.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Duties and powers.</span></h2>
          <p>The council shall have the following duties and powers:</p>
          <p>(1) to advise and otherwise aid the governor; appropriate state agencies, including but not limited to the Departments of Education, Human Services, Employment and Economic Development, and Human Rights and the Divisions of Rehabilitation Services and Services for the Blind; the state legislature; and the public on matters pertaining to public policy and the administration of programs, services, and facilities for persons who have a disability in Minnesota;</p>
          <p>(2) to encourage and assist in the development of coordinated, interdepartmental goals and objectives and the coordination of programs, services and facilities among all state departments and private providers of service as they relate to persons with a disability;</p>
          <p>(3) to serve as a source of information to the public regarding all services, programs and legislation pertaining to persons with a disability;</p>
          <p>(4) to review and make comment to the governor, state agencies, the legislature, and the public concerning adequacy of state programs, plans and budgets for services to persons with a disability and for funding under the various federal grant programs;</p>
          <p>(5) to research, formulate and advocate plans, programs and policies which will serve the needs of persons who are disabled;</p>
          <p>(6) to advise the Departments of Labor and Industry and Employment and Economic Development on the administration and improvement of the workers' compensation law as it relates to programs, facilities and personnel providing assistance to workers who are injured and disabled;</p>
          <p>(7) to advise the Workers' Compensation Division of the Department of Labor and Industry and the Workers' Compensation Court of Appeals as to the necessity and extent of any alteration or remodeling of an existing residence or the building or purchase of a new or different residence which is proposed by a licensed architect under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.137">176.137</a>;</p>
          <p>(8) to initiate or seek to intervene as a party in any administrative proceeding and judicial review thereof to protect and advance the right of all persons who are disabled to an accessible physical environment as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/326B.139">326B.139</a>; and</p>
          <p>(9) to initiate or seek to intervene as a party in any administrative or judicial proceeding which concerns programs or services provided by public or private agencies or organizations and which directly affects the legal rights of persons with a disability.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.482.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.055">16B.055, subd 2</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.482.5b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5b.<span class="headnote">Meetings.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13D.01">13D.01</a>, the Minnesota State Council on Disability may conduct a meeting of its members by telephone or other electronic means so long as the following conditions are met:</p>
          <p>(1) all members of the council participating in the meeting, wherever their physical location, can hear one another and can hear all discussion and testimony;</p>
          <p>(2) members of the public present at the regular meeting location of the council can hear all discussion and all votes of members of the council and participate in testimony;</p>
          <p>(3) at least one member of the council is physically present at the regular meeting location; and</p>
          <p>(4) all votes are conducted by roll call, so each member's vote on each issue can be identified and recorded.</p>
          <p>(b) Each member of the council participating in a meeting by telephone or other electronic means is considered present at the meeting for purposes of determining a quorum and participating in all proceedings.</p>
          <p>(c) If telephone or another electronic means is used to conduct a meeting, the council, to the extent practical, shall allow a person to monitor the meeting electronically from a remote location. The council may require the person making such a connection to pay for documented marginal costs that the council incurs as a result of the additional connection.</p>
          <p>(d) If telephone or another electronic means is used to conduct a regular, special, or emergency meeting, the council shall provide notice of the regular meeting location, of the fact that some members may participate by electronic means, and of the provisions of paragraph (c). The timing and method of providing notice is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13D.04">13D.04</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.482.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/315/#laws.0.26.0">1975 c 315 s 26</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.482.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Collection of fees.</span></h2>
          <p>The council is empowered to establish and collect fees for documents or technical services provided to the public. The fees shall be set at a level to reimburse the council for the actual cost incurred in providing the document or service. All fees collected shall be deposited into the state treasury and credited to the general fund.</p>
        </div>
