        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.482.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/33/#laws.0.2.0">2007 c 33 s 2</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.482.history
			">

      <div class="section" id="stat.256.4825">
        <h1 class="shn">256.4825 REPORT REGARDING PROGRAMS AND SERVICES FOR PEOPLE WITH DISABILITIES.</h1>
        <p>The Minnesota State Council on Disability, the Minnesota Consortium for Citizens with Disabilities, and the Arc of Minnesota may submit an annual report by January 15 of each year, beginning in 2012, to the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees with jurisdiction over programs serving people with disabilities as provided in this section. The report must describe the existing state policies and goals for programs serving people with disabilities including, but not limited to, programs for employment, transportation, housing, education, quality assurance, consumer direction, physical and programmatic access, and health. The report must provide data and measurements to assess the extent to which the policies and goals are being met. The commissioner of human services and the commissioners of other state agencies administering programs for people with disabilities shall cooperate with the Minnesota State Council on Disability, the Minnesota Consortium for Citizens with Disabilities, and the Arc of Minnesota and provide those organizations with existing published information and reports that will assist in the preparation of the report.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.4825.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/1/1/#laws.17.7.0">1Sp2010 c 1 art 17 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.256.483"><b>256.483</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/260/#laws.0.68.0">1983 c 260 s 68</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/277/#laws.0.3.0">1983 c 277 s 3</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.4835">
        <h1 class="shn">256.4835 MINNESOTA RARE DISEASE ADVISORY COUNCIL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4835.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>There is established an advisory council on rare diseases to provide advice on policies, access, equity, research, diagnosis, treatment, and education related to rare diseases. The advisory council is established in honor of Chloe Barnes and her experiences in the health care system. For purposes of this section, "rare disease" has the meaning given in United States Code, title 21, section 360bb. The council shall be called the Minnesota Rare Disease Advisory Council. The Council on Disability shall provide meeting and office space and administrative support to the advisory council but does not have authority over the work of the advisory council.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4835.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Membership.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The advisory council shall consist of at least 17 public members who reflect statewide representation. Except for initial members, members are appointed by the governor according to paragraph (b). Four members of the legislature are appointed according to paragraph (c).</p>
          <p>(b) The governor shall appoint at least the following public members according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.0597">15.0597</a>:</p>
          <p>(1) three physicians licensed and practicing in the state with experience researching, diagnosing, or treating rare diseases, including one specializing in pediatrics;</p>
          <p>(2) one registered nurse or advanced practice registered nurse licensed and practicing in the state with experience treating rare diseases;</p>
          <p>(3) at least two hospital administrators, or their designees, from hospitals in the state that provide care to persons diagnosed with a rare disease. One administrator or designee appointed under this clause must represent a hospital in which the scope of service focuses on rare diseases of pediatric patients;</p>
          <p>(4) three persons age 18 or older who either have a rare disease or are a caregiver of a person with a rare disease. One person appointed under this clause must reside in rural Minnesota;</p>
          <p>(5) a representative of a rare disease patient organization that operates in the state;</p>
          <p>(6) a social worker with experience providing services to persons diagnosed with a rare disease;</p>
          <p>(7) a pharmacist with experience with drugs used to treat rare diseases;</p>
          <p>(8) a dentist licensed and practicing in the state with experience treating rare diseases;</p>
          <p>(9) a representative of the biotechnology industry;</p>
          <p>(10) a representative of health plan companies;</p>
          <p>(11) a medical researcher with experience conducting research on rare diseases;</p>
          <p>(12) a genetic counselor with experience providing services to persons diagnosed with a rare disease or caregivers of those persons; and</p>
          <p>(13) representatives with other areas of expertise as identified by the advisory council.</p>
          <p>(c) The advisory council shall include two members of the senate, one appointed by the majority leader and one appointed by the minority leader; and two members of the house of representatives, one appointed by the speaker of the house and one appointed by the minority leader. Members appointed under this paragraph serve until their successors are appointed.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner of health or a designee, a representative of Mayo Medical School, and a representative of the University of Minnesota Medical School shall serve as ex officio, nonvoting members of the advisory council.</p>
          <p>(e) Members appointed according to paragraph (b) shall serve for a term of three years, except the initial members appointed according to paragraph (b). Members appointed according to paragraph (b) shall serve until their successors have been appointed.</p>
          <p>(f) Members may be reappointed for up to two full additional terms according to the advisory council's operating procedures.</p>
          <p>(g) Members may be removed as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.059#stat.15.059.4">15.059, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
          <p>(h) Public members serve without compensation, but may have expenses reimbursed as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.059#stat.15.059.3">15.059, subdivision 3</a>. Legislative members may receive per diem according to the rules of their respective bodies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4835.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Meetings.</span></h2>
          <p> The advisory council shall meet at the call of the chairperson or at the request of a majority of advisory council members. Meetings of the advisory council are subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13D.01">13D.01</a>, and notice of its meetings is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13D.04">13D.04</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4835.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Chairperson; executive director; staff; executive committee.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The advisory council shall elect a chairperson and other officers as it deems necessary and in accordance with the advisory council's operating procedures.</p>
          <p>(b) The advisory council shall be governed by an executive committee elected by the members of the advisory council. One member of the executive committee must be the advisory council chairperson.</p>
          <p>(c) The advisory council shall appoint an executive director. The executive director serves as an ex officio nonvoting member of the executive committee. The advisory council may delegate to the executive director any powers and duties under this section that do not require advisory council approval. The executive director serves in the unclassified service and may be removed at any time by a majority vote of the advisory council. The executive director may employ and direct staff necessary to carry out advisory council mandates, policies, activities, and objectives.</p>
          <p>(d) The executive committee may appoint additional subcommittees and work groups as necessary to fulfill the duties of the advisory council.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4835.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The advisory council's duties may include, but are not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) in conjunction with the state's medical schools, the state's schools of public health, and hospitals in the state that provide care to persons diagnosed with a rare disease, developing resources or recommendations relating to quality of and access to treatment and services in the state for persons with a rare disease, including but not limited to:</p>
          <p>(i) a list of existing, publicly accessible resources on research, diagnosis, treatment, and education relating to rare diseases;</p>
          <p>(ii) identifying best practices for rare disease care implemented in other states, at the national level, and at the international level that will improve rare disease care in the state and seeking opportunities to partner with similar organizations in other states and countries;</p>
          <p>(iii) identifying and addressing problems faced by patients with a rare disease when changing health plans, including recommendations on how to remove obstacles faced by these patients to finding a new health plan and how to improve the ease and speed of finding a new health plan that meets the needs of patients with a rare disease;</p>
          <p>(iv) identifying and addressing barriers faced by patients with a rare disease to obtaining care, caused by prior authorization requirements in private and public health plans; and</p>
          <p>(v) identifying, recommending, and implementing best practices to ensure health care providers are adequately informed of the most effective strategies for recognizing and treating rare diseases;</p>
          <p>(2) advising, consulting, and cooperating with the Department of Health, including the Advisory Committee on Heritable and Congenital Disorders; the Department of Human Services, including the Drug Utilization Review Board and the Drug Formulary Committee; and other agencies of state government in developing recommendations, information, and programs for the public and the health care community relating to diagnosis, treatment, and awareness of rare diseases;</p>
          <p>(3) advising on policy issues and advancing policy initiatives at the state and federal levels; and</p>
          <p>(4) receiving funds and issuing grants.</p>
          <p>(b) The advisory council shall collect additional topic areas for study and evaluation from the general public. In order for the advisory council to study and evaluate a topic, the topic must be approved for study and evaluation by the advisory council.</p>
          <p>(c) Legislative members may not deliberate about or vote on decisions related to the issuance of grants of state money.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4835.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Conflict of interest.</span></h2>
          <p>Advisory council members are subject to the advisory council's conflict of interest policy as outlined in the advisory council's operating procedures.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.4835.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Annual report.</span></h2>
          <p>By January 1 of each year, beginning January 1, 2020, the advisory council shall report to the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees with jurisdiction over higher education and health care policy on the advisory council's activities under subdivision 4 and other issues on which the advisory council may choose to report.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.4835.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/0/65/#laws.0.1.0">2019 c 65 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.13.2.0">2022 c 98 art 13 s 2</a>,14</p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">SOCIAL ADJUSTMENT SERVICES TO REFUGEES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.484">
        <h1 class="shn">256.484 SOCIAL ADJUSTMENT SERVICES TO REFUGEES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.484.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Special projects.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services shall establish a grant program to provide social adjustment services to refugees residing in Minnesota who experience depression, emotional stress, and personal crises resulting from past trauma and refugee camp experiences.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.484.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given them:</p>
          <p>(a) "Refugee" means a refugee or asylee status granted by the United States Citizenship and Immigration Services.</p>
          <p>(b) "Social adjustment services" means treatment or services, including psychiatric assessment, chemical therapy, individual or family counseling, support group participation, after care or follow-up, information and referral, and crisis intervention.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.484.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Project selection.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall select projects for funding under this section. Projects selected must be administered by service providers who have experience in providing bilingual social adjustment services to refugees. Project administrators must present evidence that the service provider's social adjustment services for targeted refugees has historically resolved major problems identified at the time of intake.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.484.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Project design.</span></h2>
          <p>Project proposals selected under this section must:</p>
          <p>(1) use existing resources when possible;</p>
          <p>(2) clearly specify program goals and timetables for project operation;</p>
          <p>(3) identify available support services, social services, and referral procedures to be used in serving the targeted refugees;</p>
          <p>(4) provide bilingual services; and</p>
          <p>(5) identify the training and experience that enable project staff to provide services to targeted refugees, and identify the number of staff with bilingual service expertise.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.484.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Annual report.</span></h2>
          <p>Selected service providers must report to the commissioner by June 30 of each year on the number of refugees served, the average cost per refugee served, the number and percentage of refugees who are successfully assisted through social adjustment services, and recommendations for modifications in service delivery for the upcoming year.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.484.history
			">

      </div>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.256.879"><b>256.879</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/85/#laws.1.74.0">1997 c 85 art 1 s 74</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.256.8799"><b>256.8799</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.10.98.0">2023 c 70 art 10 s 98</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">SOCIAL WELFARE FUND</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.88">
        <h1 class="shn">256.88 SOCIAL WELFARE FUND ESTABLISHED.</h1>
        <p>Except as otherwise expressly provided, all moneys and funds held by the commissioner of human services, the Direct Care and Treatment executive board, and the local social services agencies of the several counties in trust or for the benefit of children with a disability and children who are dependent, neglected, or delinquent, children born to mothers who were not married to the children's fathers at the times of the conception nor at the births of the children, persons determined to have developmental disability, mental illness, or substance use disorder, or other wards or beneficiaries, under any law, shall be kept in a single fund to be known as the "social welfare fund" which shall be deposited at interest, held, or disbursed as provided in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.89">256.89</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.92">256.92</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.88.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/25">4462</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1923/0/106/#laws.0.1.0">1923 c 106 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1939/0/8/#laws.0.1.0">1939 c 8 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/7/#laws.0.4.0">1983 c 7 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/243/#laws.0.5.0">1983 c 243 s 5</a> subd 4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.5.58.0">1984 c 654 art 5 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/631/#laws.0.31.0">1994 c 631 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/40/#laws.1.121.0">2017 c 40 art 1 s 121</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.4.51.0">2022 c 98 art 4 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/125/#laws.5.25.0">2024 c 125 art 5 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.50.25.0">2024 c 127 art 50 s 25</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.89">
        <h1 class="shn">256.89 FUND DEPOSITED IN STATE TREASURY.</h1>
        <p>The social welfare fund and all accretions thereto shall be deposited in the state treasury, as a separate and distinct fund, to the credit of the commissioner of human services and the Direct Care and Treatment executive board as trustees for their respective beneficiaries in proportion to the beneficiaries' several interests. The commissioner of management and budget shall be responsible only to the commissioner of human services and the Direct Care and Treatment executive board for the sum total of the fund, and shall have no duties nor direct obligations toward the beneficiaries thereof individually. Subject to the applicable rules of the commissioner of human services or the Direct Care and Treatment executive board, money so received by a local social services agency may be deposited by the executive secretary of the local social services agency in a local bank carrying federal deposit insurance, designated by the local social services agency for this purpose. The amount of such deposit in each such bank at any one time shall not exceed the amount protected by federal deposit insurance.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.89.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/25">4463</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1923/0/106/#laws.0.2.0">1923 c 106 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1939/0/8/#laws.0.2.0">1939 c 8 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.5.58.0">1984 c 654 art 5 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/631/#laws.0.31.0">1994 c 631 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/125/#laws.5.26.0">2024 c 125 art 5 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.50.26.0">2024 c 127 art 50 s 26</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.90">
        <h1 class="shn">256.90 SOCIAL WELFARE FUND; USE; DISPOSITION; DEPOSITORIES.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of human services, in consultation with the Direct Care and Treatment executive board, at least 30 days before the first day of January and the first day of July in each year shall file with the commissioner of management and budget an estimate of the amount of the social welfare fund to be held in the treasury during the succeeding six-month period, subject to current disbursement. Such portion of the remainder thereof as may be at any time designated by the request of the commissioner of human services may be invested by the commissioner of management and budget in bonds in which the permanent trust funds of the state of Minnesota may be invested, upon approval by the State Board of Investment. The portion of such remainder not so invested shall be placed by the commissioner of management and budget at interest for the period of six months, or when directed by the commissioner of human services, for the period of 12 months thereafter at the highest rate of interest obtainable in a bank, or banks, designated by the board of deposit as a suitable depository therefor. All the provisions of law relative to the designation and qualification of depositories of other state funds shall be applicable to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.88">256.88</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.92">256.92</a>, except as herein otherwise provided. Any bond given, or collateral assigned or both, to secure a deposit hereunder may be continuous in character to provide for the repayment of any moneys belonging to the fund theretofore or thereafter at any time deposited in such bank until its designation as such depository is revoked and the security thereof shall be not impaired by any subsequent agreement or understanding as to the rate of interest to be paid upon such deposit, or as to time for its repayment. The amount of money belonging to the fund deposited in any bank, including other state deposits, shall not at any time exceed the amount of the capital stock thereof. In the event of the closing of the bank any sum deposited therein shall immediately become due and payable.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.90.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/25">4464</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1923/0/106/#laws.0.3.0">1923 c 106 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1925/0/253/#laws.0.0.0">1925 c 253</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1943/0/236/#laws.0.1.0">1943 c 236 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.5.58.0">1984 c 654 art 5 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/125/#laws.5.27.0">2024 c 125 art 5 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.50.27.0">2024 c 127 art 50 s 27</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.91">
        <h1 class="shn">256.91 PURPOSES.</h1>
        <p>From that part of the social welfare fund held in the state treasury subject to disbursement as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.90">256.90</a> the commissioner of human services or the Direct Care and Treatment executive board at any time may pay out such amounts as the commissioner or executive board deems proper for the support, maintenance, or other legal benefit of any of the children with a disability and children who are dependent, neglected, or delinquent, children born to mothers who were not married to the children's fathers at the times of the conception nor at the births of the children, persons with developmental disability, substance use disorder, or mental illness, or other wards or persons entitled thereto, not exceeding in the aggregate to or for any person the principal amount previously received for the benefit of the person, together with the increase in it from an equitable apportionment of interest realized from the social welfare fund.</p>
        <p>When any such person dies or is finally discharged from the guardianship, care, custody, and control of the commissioner of human services or the Direct Care and Treatment executive board, the amount then remaining subject to use for the benefit of the person shall be paid as soon as may be from the social welfare fund to the persons thereto entitled by law.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.91.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/25">4465</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1923/0/106/#laws.0.4.0">1923 c 106 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/7/#laws.0.5.0">1983 c 7 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/243/#laws.0.5.0">1983 c 243 s 5</a> subd 5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.5.58.0">1984 c 654 art 5 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/21/#laws.0.50.0">1985 c 21 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/40/#laws.1.121.0">2017 c 40 art 1 s 121</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.4.51.0">2022 c 98 art 4 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/125/#laws.5.28.0">2024 c 125 art 5 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.50.28.0">2024 c 127 art 50 s 28</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.92">
        <h1 class="shn">256.92 COMMISSIONER OF HUMAN SERVICES AND DIRECT CARE AND TREATMENT, ACCOUNTS.</h1>
        <p>It shall be the duty of the commissioner of human services, the Direct Care and Treatment executive board, and the local social services agencies of the several counties of this state to cause to be deposited with the commissioner of management and budget all moneys and funds in their possession or under their control and designated by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.91">256.91</a> as and for the social welfare fund; and all such moneys and funds shall be so deposited in the state treasury as soon as received. The commissioner of human services, in consultation with the Direct Care and Treatment executive board, shall keep books of account or other records showing separately the principal amount received and deposited in the social welfare fund for the benefit of any person, together with the name of such person, and the name and address, if known to the commissioner of human services or the Direct Care and Treatment executive board, of the person from whom such money was received; and, at least once every two years, the amount of interest, if any, which the money has earned in the social welfare fund shall be apportioned thereto and posted in the books of account or records to the credit of such beneficiary.</p>
        <p>The provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.88">256.88</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.92">256.92</a> shall not apply to any fund or money now or hereafter deposited or otherwise disposed of pursuant to the lawful orders, decrees, judgments, or other directions of any district court having jurisdiction thereof.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.92.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/25">4466</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/25">4467</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1923/0/106/#laws.0.5.0">1923 c 106 s 5</a>,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.5.58.0">1984 c 654 art 5 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/631/#laws.0.31.0">1994 c 631 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/189/#laws.0.8.0">1995 c 189 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/277/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 277 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/125/#laws.5.29.0">2024 c 125 art 5 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.50.29.0">2024 c 127 art 50 s 29</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.925">
        <h1 class="shn">256.925 OPTIONAL VOTER REGISTRATION FOR PUBLIC ASSISTANCE APPLICANTS AND RECIPIENTS.</h1>
        <p>A county agency shall provide voter registration cards to every individual eligible to vote who applies for a public assistance program at the time application is made. The agency shall also make voter registration cards available to a public assistance recipient upon the recipient's request or at the time of the recipient's eligibility redetermination. The county agency shall assist applicants and recipients in completing the voter registration cards, as needed. Applicants must be informed that completion of the cards is optional. Completed forms shall be collected by agency employees and submitted to proper election officials.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.925.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/689/#laws.2.136.0">1988 c 689 art 2 s 136</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.93">
        <h1 class="shn">256.93 COMMISSIONER OF HUMAN SERVICES, POSSESSION OF ESTATES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.93.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Limitations.</span></h2>
          <p>In any case where the guardianship of any child with a developmental disability or who is disabled, dependent, neglected or delinquent, or a child born to a mother who was not married to the child's father when the child was conceived nor when the child was born, has been committed to the commissioner of human services, and in any case where the guardianship of any person with a developmental disability has been committed to the commissioner of human services, the court having jurisdiction of the estate may on such notice as the court may direct, authorize the commissioner to take possession of the personal property in the estate, liquidate it, and hold the proceeds in trust for the ward, to be invested, expended and accounted for as provided by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.88">256.88</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.92">256.92</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.93.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Annual report.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services shall annually or at such other times as the court may direct file with the court an account of moneys received and disbursed by the commissioner for wards and conservatees, pursuant to subdivision 1. Upon petition of the ward or conservatee or of any person interested in such estate and upon notice to the commissioner the court may terminate such trust and require final accounting thereof.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.93.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/25">4467-1</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/25">4467-2</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1929/0/55/#laws.0.1.0">1929 c 55 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1939/0/9/#laws.0.0.0">1939 c 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1943/0/612/#laws.0.4.0">1943 c 612 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1949/0/32/#laws.0.1.0">1949 c 32 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/208/#laws.0.31.0">1975 c 208 s 31</a>,32; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/7/#laws.0.6.0">1983 c 7 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/10/#laws.0.1.0">1983 c 10 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/243/#laws.0.5.0">1983 c 243 s 5</a> subd 6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.5.58.0">1984 c 654 art 5 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/21/#laws.0.51.0">1985 c 21 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/189/#laws.0.8.0">1995 c 189 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/277/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 277 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/10/#laws.4.13.0">2005 c 10 art 4 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/40/#laws.1.121.0">2017 c 40 art 1 s 121</a></p>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.256.9364"><b>256.9364</b> [Expired]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.9365">
        <h1 class="shn">256.9365 PURCHASE OF HEALTH CARE COVERAGE FOR PEOPLE LIVING WITH HIV.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9365.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Program established.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services shall establish a program to pay the cost of health plan premiums and cost sharing for prescriptions, including co-payments, deductibles, and coinsurance for persons who have contracted human immunodeficiency virus (HIV) to enable them to continue coverage under or enroll in a group or individual health plan. If a person is determined to be eligible under subdivision 2, the commissioner shall pay the health insurance premiums and prescription cost sharing, including co-payments and deductibles required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0631">256B.0631</a>. The commissioner shall not pay for that portion of a premium that is attributable to other family members or dependents or is paid by the individual's employer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9365.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligibility requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>To be eligible for the program, an applicant must meet all eligibility requirements for Part B of the Ryan White HIV/AIDS Treatment Extension Act of 2009, Public Law 111-87, and enroll in the Minnesota Ryan White program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9365.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Cost-effective coverage.</span></h2>
          <p>Requirements for the payment of individual plan premiums under this section must be designed to ensure that the state cost of paying an individual plan premium does not exceed the estimated state cost that would otherwise be incurred in the medical assistance program. The commissioner shall purchase the most cost-effective coverage available for eligible individuals.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.9365.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/568/#laws.3.15.0">1990 c 568 art 3 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.4.18.0">1991 c 292 art 4 s 18</a>,19; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/207/#laws.6.13.0">1995 c 207 art 6 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.2.64.0">2016 c 158 art 2 s 64</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/59/#laws.0.7.0">2017 c 59 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.5.33.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 5 s 33</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.256.9366"><b>256.9366</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256L.13">256L.13</a>]
</div>
