Expenditures shall be made (1) to strengthen and expand existing retired senior volunteer programs, and (2) to encourage the development of new programs in areas in the state where these programs do not exist. Grants shall be made consistent with applicable federal guidelines.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9753.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.4.9.0">2014 c 262 art 4 s 9</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.9753.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/455/#laws.0.1.0">1980 c 455 s 1</a>-4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/305/#laws.1.57.0">1996 c 305 art 1 s 57</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/220/#laws.10.33.0">2002 c 220 art 10 s 33</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.9754">
        <h1 class="shn">256.9754 LIVE WELL AT HOME GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9754.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(a) "Community" means a town, township, city, or targeted neighborhood within a city, or a consortium of towns, townships, cities, or targeted neighborhoods within cities.</p>
          <p>(b) "Core home and community-based services provider" means a Faith in Action, Living at Home/Block Nurse, congregational nurse, or similar community-based program governed by a board, the majority of whose members reside within the program's service area, that organizes and uses volunteers and paid staff to deliver nonmedical services intended to assist older adults to identify and manage risks and to maintain the older adults' community living and integration in the community.</p>
          <p>(c) "Long-term services and supports" means any service available under the elderly waiver program or alternative care grant programs, nursing facility services, transportation services, caregiver support and respite care services, and other home and community-based services identified as necessary either to maintain lifestyle choices for older adults or to support older adults to remain in their own home.</p>
          <p>(d) "Older adult services" means any services available under the elderly waiver program or alternative care grant programs; nursing facility services; transportation services; respite services; and other community-based services identified as necessary either to maintain lifestyle choices for older Minnesotans, or to promote independence.</p>
          <p>(e) "Older adult" refers to individuals 65 years of age and older.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9754.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Creation; purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The live well at home grants are created under the administration of the commissioner of human services.</p>
          <p>(b) The purpose of projects selected by the commissioner of human services under this section is to make strategic changes in the long-term services and supports system for older adults and people with dementia, including statewide capacity for local service development and technical assistance and statewide availability of home and community-based services for older adult services, caregiver support and respite care services, and other supports in Minnesota. These projects are intended to create incentives for new and expanded home and community-based services in Minnesota in order to:</p>
          <p>(1) reach older adults early in the progression of older adults' need for long-term services and supports, providing them with low-cost, high-impact services that will prevent or delay the use of more costly services;</p>
          <p>(2) support older adults to live in the most integrated, least restrictive community setting;</p>
          <p>(3) support the informal caregivers of older adults;</p>
          <p>(4) develop and implement strategies to integrate long-term services and supports with health care services, in order to improve the quality of care and enhance the quality of life of older adults and older adults' informal caregivers;</p>
          <p>(5) ensure cost-effective use of financial and human resources;</p>
          <p>(6) build community-based approaches and community commitment to delivering long-term services and supports for older adults in their own homes;</p>
          <p>(7) achieve a broad awareness and use of lower-cost in-home services as an alternative to nursing homes and other residential services;</p>
          <p>(8) strengthen and develop additional home and community-based services and alternatives to nursing homes and other residential services; and</p>
          <p>(9) strengthen programs that use volunteers.</p>
          <p>(c) The services provided by these projects are available to older adults who are eligible for medical assistance and the elderly waiver under chapter 256S, the alternative care program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0913">256B.0913</a>, or the essential community supports grant under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0922">256B.0922</a>, and to older adults who have their own money to pay for services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9754.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Community services development grants.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall make community services development grants available to communities, providers of older adult services, or a consortium of providers of older adult services to establish older adult services. Grants may be provided for capital and other costs including, but not limited to, start-up and training costs, equipment, and supplies related to older adult services or other residential or service alternatives to nursing facility care. Grants may also be made to renovate current buildings, provide transportation services, fund programs that would allow older adults or individuals with a disability to stay in their own homes by sharing a home, fund programs that coordinate and manage formal and informal services to older adults in their homes to enable them to live as independently as possible in their own homes as an alternative to nursing home care, or expand state-funded programs in the area.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9754.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Priority for other grants.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of health shall give priority to a grantee selected under subdivision 3 when awarding technology-related grants, if the grantee is using technology as part of the proposal unless that priority conflicts with existing state or federal guidance related to grant awards by the Department of Health. The commissioner of transportation shall give priority to a grantee under subdivision 3 when distributing transportation-related funds to create transportation options for older adults unless that preference conflicts with existing state or federal guidance related to grant awards by the Department of Transportation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9754.3b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3b.<span class="headnote">State waivers.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of health may waive applicable state laws and rules for grantees under subdivision 3 on a time-limited basis if the commissioner of health determines that a participating grantee requires a waiver in order to achieve demonstration project goals.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9754.3c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3c.<span class="headnote">Caregiver support and respite care projects.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall establish projects to expand the availability of caregiver support and respite care services for family and other caregivers. The commissioner shall use a request for proposals to select nonprofit entities to administer the projects. Projects must:</p>
          <p>(1) establish a local coordinated network of volunteer and paid respite workers;</p>
          <p>(2) coordinate assignment of respite care services to caregivers of older adults;</p>
          <p>(3) assure the health and safety of the older adults;</p>
          <p>(4) identify at-risk caregivers;</p>
          <p>(5) provide information, education, and training for caregivers in the designated community; and</p>
          <p>(6) demonstrate the need in the proposed service area, particularly where nursing facility closures have occurred or are occurring or areas with service needs identified by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144A.351">144A.351</a>. Preference must be given for projects that reach underserved populations.</p>
          <p>(b) Projects must clearly describe:</p>
          <p>(1) how they will achieve their purpose;</p>
          <p>(2) the process for recruiting, training, and retraining volunteers; and</p>
          <p>(3) a plan to promote the project in the designated community, including outreach to older adults needing the services.</p>
          <p>(c) Money for all projects under this subdivision may be used to:</p>
          <p>(1) hire a coordinator to develop a coordinated network of volunteer and paid respite care services and assign workers to clients;</p>
          <p>(2) recruit and train volunteer providers;</p>
          <p>(3) provide information, training, and education to caregivers;</p>
          <p>(4) advertise the availability of the caregiver support and respite care project; and</p>
          <p>(5) purchase equipment to maintain a system of assigning workers to clients.</p>
          <p>(d) Volunteer and caregiver training must include resources on how to support an individual with dementia.</p>
          <p>(e) Project money may not be used to supplant existing funding sources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9754.3d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3d.<span class="headnote">Core home and community-based services projects.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall select and contract with core home and community-based services providers for projects to provide services and supports to older adults both with and without family and other informal caregivers using a request for proposals process. Projects must:</p>
          <p>(1) have a credible public or private nonprofit sponsor providing ongoing financial support;</p>
          <p>(2) have a specific, clearly defined geographic service area;</p>
          <p>(3) use a practice framework designed to identify high-risk older adults and help them take action to better manage their chronic conditions and maintain their community living;</p>
          <p>(4) have a team approach to coordination and care, ensuring that the older adult participants, participants' families, and the formal and informal providers are all part of planning and providing services;</p>
          <p>(5) provide information, support services, homemaking services, counseling, and training for the older adults and family caregivers;</p>
          <p>(6) encourage service area or neighborhood residents and local organizations to collaborate in meeting the needs of older adults in their geographic service areas;</p>
          <p>(7) recruit, train, and direct the use of volunteers to provide informal services and other appropriate support to older adults and their caregivers; and</p>
          <p>(8) provide coordination and management of formal and informal services to older adults and older adults' families using less expensive alternatives.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9754.3e">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3e.<span class="headnote">Community service grants.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall award contracts for grants to public and private nonprofit agencies to establish services that strengthen a community's ability to provide a system of home and community-based services for elderly persons. The commissioner shall use a request for proposals process.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9754.3f">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3f.<span class="headnote">Live Well at Home grant extensions. </span></h2>
          <p>(a) A current grantee under subdivision 3, 3c, 3d, or 3e may apply to the commissioner to receive on a noncompetitive basis up to two years of additional funding.</p>
          <p>(b) To be eligible for a grant extension, a grant extension applicant must have been awarded a grant under this section within the previous five years and provide at least one eligible service in an underserved community. The grantee must submit to the commissioner a letter of intent to continue providing the eligible service after the expiration of a grant extension provided under this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner of human services must give priority to submitted letters of intent from grantees who have demonstrated success in providing chore services, homemaker services, transportation services, grocery services, caregiver supports, service coordination, or other home and community-based services to older adults in underserved communities.</p>
          <p>(d) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.98#stat.16B.98.5">16B.98, subdivision 5</a>, paragraph (b), the commissioner may from within available appropriations extend a grant agreement up to two additional years, not to exceed seven years, for grantees the commissioner determines can successfully sustain the grantee's Live Well at Home project with the additional funds made available through the grant agreement extension.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9754.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>Grants may be awarded only to communities and providers or to a consortium of providers that have a local match of 50 percent of the costs for the project in the form of donations, local tax dollars, in-kind donations, fundraising, or other local matches.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9754.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Grant preference.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services shall give preference when awarding grants under this section to areas where nursing facility closures have occurred or are occurring or areas with service needs identified by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144A.351">144A.351</a>. The commissioner may award grants to the extent grant funds are available and to the extent applications are approved by the commissioner. Denial of approval of an application in one year does not preclude submission of an application in a subsequent year. The maximum grant amount is limited to $750,000.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.9754.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/9/#laws.4.3.0">1Sp2001 c 9 art 4 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.1.113.0">2002 c 379 art 1 s 113</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.2.11.0">2013 c 108 art 2 s 11</a>-13,44; art 15 s 3,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/40/#laws.1.121.0">2017 c 40 art 1 s 121</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/61/#laws.2.2.0">2023 c 61 art 2 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.9755">
        <h1 class="shn">256.9755 CAREGIVER SUPPORT PROGRAMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9755.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Program goals.</span></h2>
          <p>It is a goal of all area agencies on aging and caregiver support programs to support family caregivers of persons with amyotrophic lateral sclerosis (ALS) who are living in the community by:</p>
          <p>(1) promoting caregiver support programs that serve Minnesotans in their homes and communities;</p>
          <p>(2) providing, within the limits of available funds, the caregiver support services that enable the family caregiver to access caregiver support programs in the most cost-effective and efficient manner; and</p>
          <p>(3) providing information, education, and training to respite caregivers and volunteers about caring for, managing, and coping with care for a person with ALS.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9755.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The Minnesota Board on Aging shall allocate the state funds that are received under this section for the caregiver support program. The board shall give priority to those areas where there is a high need of respite services as evidenced by the data provided by the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9755.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Caregiver support services.</span></h2>
          <p>Funds allocated under this section for caregiver support services must be used to reach family caregivers of persons with ALS and such funds may be used to provide services benefiting people under the age of 60 and their caregivers. The funds must be used to provide social, community-based services and activities that provide social interaction for participants. The funds may also be used to provide respite care.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9755.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Report.</span></h2>
          <p>By January 15, 2025, and every other January 15 thereafter, the Minnesota Board on Aging shall submit a progress report about the caregiver support grants in this section to the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees and divisions with jurisdiction over human services. The progress report must include metrics of the use of the grant program.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.9755.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/42/#laws.0.1.0">2022 c 42 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/125/#laws.2.8.0">2024 c 125 art 2 s 8</a>,9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.47.8.0">2024 c 127 art 47 s 8</a>,9</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.9756">
        <h1 class="shn">256.9756 CAREGIVER RESPITE SERVICES GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9756.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Caregiver respite services grant program established.</span></h2>
          <p>The Minnesota Board on Aging must establish a caregiver respite services grant program to increase the availability of respite services for family caregivers of people with dementia and to provide information, education, and training to respite caregivers and volunteers regarding caring for people with dementia. From the money made available for this purpose, the board must award grants on a competitive basis to respite service providers, giving priority to areas of the state where there is a high need of respite services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9756.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligible uses.</span></h2>
          <p>Grant recipients awarded grant money under this section must use a portion of the grant award as determined by the board to provide free or subsidized respite services for family caregivers of people with dementia.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9756.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Report.</span></h2>
          <p>By January 15, 2026, the board shall submit a progress report about the caregiver respite services grants in this section to the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees and divisions with jurisdiction over human services finance and policy. The progress report must include metrics of the use of grant program money. This subdivision expires upon submission of the report. The board shall notify the revisor of statutes when the report is submitted.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.9756.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/61/#laws.2.3.0">2023 c 61 art 2 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/125/#laws.2.10.0">2024 c 125 art 2 s 10</a>,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.47.10.0">2024 c 127 art 47 s 10</a>,11</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.976">
        <h1 class="shn">256.976 FOSTER GRANDPARENTS PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.976.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Program established.</span></h2>
          <p>There is established a foster grandparents program which will engage the services of low-income persons aged 60 or over to provide supportive person to person assistance in health, education, welfare, and related fields to persons receiving care in resident group homes for dependent and neglected persons, day care centers or other public or private nonprofit institutions or agencies providing care for neglected and disadvantaged persons who lack close personal relationships.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.976.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Compensation.</span></h2>
          <p>Persons employed as foster grandparents shall be compensated for no more than 20 hours per week and at an hourly rate not to exceed the federal minimum wage by more than 20 percent. In addition to such compensation foster grandparents shall be eligible for protective clothing, including replacement of glasses; transportation assistance, not to exceed mileage payments for 20 miles per day or chartered transportation service, for travel between residence and place of employment; workers' compensation; annual physical examinations; food services during employment, generally provided by the employing agency or institution; and such other assistance as the Minnesota Board on Aging may prescribe. No person employed as a foster grandparent shall be terminated because of redefinition of income standards, or a change of income, marital status, or number of dependents.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.976.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Grants-in-aid.</span></h2>
          <p>The Minnesota Board on Aging, hereinafter called the board, may make grants-in-aid for the employment of foster grandparents to qualified resident group homes for dependent and neglected persons, day care centers and other public or nonprofit private institutions and agencies providing care for neglected and disadvantaged persons who lack close personal relationships. Agencies and institutions seeking aid shall apply on a form prescribed by the board. Priority shall be given to agencies and institutions providing care for children with developmental disabilities. Grants shall not be made to local public or nonprofit agencies until 40 percent of the recognized need for foster grandparents within state institutions has been met. Grants shall be for a period of 12 months or less, and grants to local public and nonprofit agencies or institutions shall be based on 90 percent state, and ten percent local sharing of program expenditures authorized by the board. Grants shall not be used to match other state funds nor shall any person paid from grant funds be used to replace any staff member of the grantee. Grants may be used to match federal funds. Each grantee shall file a semiannual report with the board at the time and containing such information as the board shall prescribe.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.976.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Rulemaking authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The board is authorized, subject to the provisions of chapter 14, to make rules necessary to the operation of the foster grandparent program and to employ assistance in performing its administrative duties. In adopting rules the board shall give consideration to applicable federal guidelines.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.976.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/938/#laws.0.1.0">1971 c 938 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/302/#laws.0.1.0">1973 c 302 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/359/#laws.0.23.0">1975 c 359 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/216/#laws.1.39.0">1983 c 216 art 1 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/59/#laws.3.2.0">2013 c 59 art 3 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.977">
        <h1 class="shn">256.977 SENIOR COMPANION PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.977.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Citation.</span></h2>
          <p>This section may be cited as the "Minnesota Senior Companion Act."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.977.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Establishment of program.</span></h2>
          <p>There is established a senior companion program to engage the services of low-income persons aged 60 or over to provide supportive person to person assistance in health, education, welfare and related fields primarily to adults with a disability and elderly people living in their own homes. Senior companions may also be used to provide such services to adults with a disability and elderly persons living or receiving care in resident group homes for dependent and neglected persons, nursing homes, private homes, or other public or private nonprofit institutions or agencies providing care for adults with a disability or elderly persons. Foster grandparents currently serving individuals over 21 years of age pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.976">256.976</a> shall, after July 1, 1976, be called senior companions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.977.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Compensation.</span></h2>
          <p>Persons serving as senior companions shall be compensated for no more than 20 hours per week at an hourly rate not to exceed the rate established under the Older Americans Act. In addition, senior companions shall receive such other assistance as the Minnesota Board on Aging may prescribe. No person serving as a senior companion shall be terminated as a result of a change in the eligibility requirements set by the Minnesota Board on Aging, nor as a result of a change in income, marital status, or number of dependents.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.977.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Grants.</span></h2>
          <p>The Minnesota Board on Aging may make grants-in-aid for the purchase of senior companion services by nonprofit agencies and institutions and individuals who have access to or responsibility for adults with a disability and the elderly. Applications to provide senior companion services to individuals in their homes shall have priority over applications to provide services to individuals living in group homes, nursing homes, or other institutions. Applications for grants shall be made on forms prescribed by the Minnesota Board on Aging.</p>
          <p>Grants shall be paid as follows: 90 percent of the program expenditures authorized by the Minnesota Board on Aging shall be paid by the state and ten percent shall be paid by local matching funds. Grants shall be for a period of 12 months or less. Grants shall not be used to match other state funds nor shall any person paid from grant funds be used to replace any staff members of the grantee. Each grantee shall file a semiannual report with the Minnesota Board on Aging at the time and containing the information as the board shall prescribe.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.977.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The Minnesota Board on Aging shall promulgate rules necessary to implement the provisions of this section and may employ necessary assistance in performing its administrative duties. Rules adopted shall be consistent with applicable federal guidelines.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.977.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/323/#laws.0.1.0">1976 c 323 s 1</a>-2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/40/#laws.1.121.0">2017 c 40 art 1 s 121</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.256.9772"><b>256.9772</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.2.57.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 2 s 57</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.256.978"><b>256.978</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/518A.83">518A.83</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.256.979"><b>256.979</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/518A.84">518A.84</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.256.9791"><b>256.9791</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/9/#laws.1.35.0">1Sp2011 c 9 art 1 s 35</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.256.9792"><b>256.9792</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.1.12.0">2014 c 262 art 1 s 12</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.98">
        <h1 class="shn">256.98 WRONGFULLY OBTAINING ASSISTANCE; THEFT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.98.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Wrongfully obtaining assistance.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person who commits any of the following acts or omissions with intent to defeat the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.891">145.891</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.897">145.897</a>, the MFIP program formerly codified in sections 256.031 to 256.0361, the AFDC program formerly codified in sections 256.72 to 256.871, chapter 142G, 256B, 256D, 256I, 256K, or 256L, child care assistance programs, and emergency assistance programs under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256D.06">256D.06</a>, is guilty of theft and shall be sentenced under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.52#stat.609.52.3">609.52, subdivision 3</a>, clauses (1) to (5):</p>
          <p>(1) obtains or attempts to obtain, or aids or abets any person to obtain by means of a willfully false statement or representation, by intentional concealment of any material fact, or by impersonation or other fraudulent device, assistance or the continued receipt of assistance, to include child care assistance or food benefits produced according to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.891">145.891</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.897">145.897</a> and MinnesotaCare services according to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.9365">256.9365</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.94">256.94</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256L.01">256L.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256L.15">256L.15</a>, to which the person is not entitled or assistance greater than that to which the person is entitled;</p>
          <p>(2) knowingly aids or abets in buying or in any way disposing of the property of a recipient or applicant of assistance without the consent of the county agency; or</p>
          <p>(3) obtains or attempts to obtain, alone or in collusion with others, the receipt of payments to which the individual is not entitled as a provider of subsidized child care, or by furnishing or concurring in a willfully false claim for child care assistance.</p>
          <p>(b) The continued receipt of assistance to which the person is not entitled or greater than that to which the person is entitled as a result of any of the acts, failure to act, or concealment described in this subdivision shall be deemed to be continuing offenses from the date that the first act or failure to act occurred.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.98.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Joint trials.</span></h2>
          <p>When two or more defendants are jointly charged with the same offense under subdivision 1, or are jointly charged with different offenses under subdivision 1 arising from the same course of conduct, they shall be tried jointly; however, if it appears to the court that a defendant or the state is substantially prejudiced by the joinder for trial, the court may order an election or separate trial of counts, grant a severance of defendants, or provide other relief.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.98.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Amount of assistance incorrectly paid.</span></h2>
          <p>The amount of the assistance incorrectly paid under this section is:</p>
          <p>(1) the difference between the amount of assistance actually received on the basis of misrepresented or concealed facts and the amount to which the recipient would have been entitled had the specific concealment or misrepresentation not occurred. Unless required by law, rule, or regulation, earned income disregards shall not be applied to earnings not reported by the recipient; or</p>
          <p>(2) equal to all payments for health care services, including capitation payments made to a health plan, made on behalf of a person enrolled in MinnesotaCare, medical assistance, or general assistance medical care formerly codified in chapter 256D, for which the person was not entitled due to the concealment or misrepresentation of facts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.98.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Recovery of assistance.</span></h2>
          <p>The amount of assistance determined to have been incorrectly paid is recoverable from:</p>
          <p>(1) the recipient or the recipient's estate by the county or the state as a debt due the county or the state or both; and</p>
          <p>(2) any person found to have taken independent action to establish eligibility for, conspired with, or aided and abetted, any recipient of public assistance found to have been incorrectly paid.</p>
          <p>The obligations established under this subdivision shall be joint and several and shall extend to all cases involving client error as well as cases involving wrongfully obtained assistance.</p>
          <p>MinnesotaCare participants who have been found to have wrongfully obtained assistance as described in subdivision 1, but who otherwise remain eligible for the program, may agree to have their MinnesotaCare premiums increased by an amount equal to ten percent of their premiums or $10 per month, whichever is greater, until the debt is satisfied.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.98.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Criminal or civil action.</span></h2>
          <p>To prosecute or to recover assistance wrongfully obtained under this section, the attorney general or the appropriate county attorney, acting independently or at the direction of the attorney general, may institute a criminal or civil action or both.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.98.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Rule superseded.</span></h2>
          <p><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/court_rules/cr/id/17/#17.03.Subd._2.">Rule 17.03, subdivision 2</a>, of the Minnesota Rules of Criminal Procedure that relates to joint trials is superseded by this section to the extent that it conflicts with this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.98.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Division of recovered amounts.</span></h2>
          <p>Except for recoveries under chapter 142E, if the state is responsible for the recovery, the amounts recovered shall be paid to the appropriate units of government. If the recovery is directly attributable to a county, the county may retain one-half of the nonfederal share of any recovery from a recipient or the recipient's estate.</p>
          <p>This subdivision does not apply to recoveries from medical providers or to recoveries involving the department of human services, surveillance and utilization review division, state hospital collections unit, and the benefit recoveries division.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.98.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Disqualification from program.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Any person found to be guilty of wrongfully obtaining assistance by a federal or state court or by an administrative hearing determination, or waiver thereof, through a disqualification consent agreement, or as part of any approved diversion plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/401.065">401.065</a>, or any court-ordered stay which carries with it any probationary or other conditions, in the Minnesota family investment program and any affiliated program to include the work participation cash benefit program, the Supplemental Nutrition Assistance Program (SNAP), the general assistance program, housing support under chapter 256I, or the Minnesota supplemental aid program shall be disqualified from that program. In addition, any person disqualified from the Minnesota family investment program shall also be disqualified from SNAP. The needs of that individual shall not be taken into consideration in determining the grant level for that assistance unit:</p>
          <p>(1) for one year after the first offense;</p>
          <p>(2) for two years after the second offense; and</p>
          <p>(3) permanently after the third or subsequent offense.</p>
          <p>The period of program disqualification shall begin on the date stipulated on the advance notice of disqualification without possibility of postponement for administrative stay or administrative hearing and shall continue through completion unless and until the findings upon which the sanctions were imposed are reversed by a court of competent jurisdiction. The period for which sanctions are imposed is not subject to review. The sanctions provided under this subdivision are in addition to, and not in substitution for, any other sanctions that may be provided for by law for the offense involved. A disqualification established through hearing or waiver shall result in the disqualification period beginning immediately unless the person has become otherwise ineligible for assistance. If the person is ineligible for assistance, the disqualification period begins when the person again meets the eligibility criteria of the program from which they were disqualified and makes application for that program.</p>
          <p>(b) A family receiving assistance through child care assistance programs under chapter 142E with a family member who is found to be guilty of wrongfully obtaining child care assistance by a federal court, state court, or an administrative hearing determination or waiver, through a disqualification consent agreement, as part of an approved diversion plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/401.065">401.065</a>, or a court-ordered stay with probationary or other conditions, is disqualified from child care assistance programs. The disqualifications must be for periods of one year and two years for the first and second offenses, respectively. Subsequent violations must result in permanent disqualification. During the disqualification period, disqualification from any child care program must extend to all child care programs and must be immediately applied.</p>
          <p>(c) A provider caring for children receiving assistance through child care assistance programs under chapter 142E is disqualified from receiving payment for child care services from the child care assistance program under chapter 142E when the provider is found to have wrongfully obtained child care assistance by a federal court, state court, or an administrative hearing determination or waiver under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.27">142A.27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142E.51#stat.142E.51.5">142E.51, subdivision 5</a>; or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.046">256.046</a>, through a disqualification consent agreement, as part of an approved diversion plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/401.065">401.065</a>, or a court-ordered stay with probationary or other conditions. The disqualification must be for a period of three years for the first offense. Any subsequent violation must result in permanent disqualification. The disqualification period must be imposed immediately after a determination is made under this paragraph. During the disqualification period, the provider is disqualified from receiving payment from any child care program under chapter 142E.</p>
          <p>(d) Any person found to be guilty of wrongfully obtaining MinnesotaCare for adults without children and upon federal approval, all categories of medical assistance and remaining categories of MinnesotaCare, except for children through age 18, by a federal or state court or by an administrative hearing determination, or waiver thereof, through a disqualification consent agreement, or as part of any approved diversion plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/401.065">401.065</a>, or any court-ordered stay which carries with it any probationary or other conditions, is disqualified from that program. The period of disqualification is one year after the first offense, two years after the second offense, and permanently after the third or subsequent offense. The period of program disqualification shall begin on the date stipulated on the advance notice of disqualification without possibility of postponement for administrative stay or administrative hearing and shall continue through completion unless and until the findings upon which the sanctions were imposed are reversed by a court of competent jurisdiction. The period for which sanctions are imposed is not subject to review. The sanctions provided under this subdivision are in addition to, and not in substitution for, any other sanctions that may be provided for by law for the offense involved.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.98.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Welfare reform coverage.</span></h2>
          <p>All references to MFIP or Minnesota family investment program contained in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.017">256.017</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.019">256.019</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.045">256.045</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.046">256.046</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.98">256.98</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.9866">256.9866</a> shall be construed to include all variations of the Minnesota family investment program including, but not limited to, chapter 142G, MFIP, MFIP-R, and chapter 256K.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.98.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/550/#laws.0.1.0">1971 c 550 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/348/#laws.0.1.0">1973 c 348 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/717/#laws.0.16.0">1973 c 717 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/437/#laws.2.2.0">1975 c 437 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/225/#laws.0.1.0">1977 c 225 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/254/#laws.0.6.0">1987 c 254 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/403/#laws.2.72.0">1987 c 403 art 2 s 72</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/712/#laws.0.2.0">1988 c 712 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/566/#laws.0.6.0">1990 c 566 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/568/#laws.4.84.0">1990 c 568 art 4 s 84</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.5.26.0">1991 c 292 art 5 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/513/#laws.8.14.0">1992 c 513 art 8 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/207/#laws.2.30.0">1995 c 207 art 2 s 30</a>,31; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/85/#laws.5.8.0">1997 c 85 art 5 s 8</a>-10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/5/#laws.0.14.0">1Sp1997 c 5 s 14</a>,15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/159/#laws.0.46.0">1999 c 159 s 46</a>,47; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/205/#laws.1.54.0">1999 c 205 art 1 s 54</a>-56; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/9/#laws.10.2.0">1Sp2001 c 9 art 10 s 2</a>,66; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.1.113.0">2002 c 379 art 1 s 113</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.1.106.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 1 s 106</a>; art 9 s 33; art 12 s 12-14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/288/#laws.4.26.0">2004 c 288 art 4 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/277/#laws.1.34.0">2008 c 277 art 1 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/301/#laws.1.2.0">2010 c 301 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.3.23.0">2013 c 108 art 3 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/78/#laws.4.51.0">2015 c 78 art 4 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.2.69.0">2016 c 158 art 2 s 69</a>,70; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.2.39.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 2 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.1.42.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 1 s 42</a>; art 2 s 108,109; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.3.43.0">2021 c 30 art 3 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.10.16.0">2023 c 70 art 10 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.78.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 78</a>; art 4 s 26; art 5 s 7; art 7 s 12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.42.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 42</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to subdivision 8 by Laws 2023, chapter 70, article 10, section 16, is effective March 1, 2026, and applies to acts of wrongfully obtained assistance that occur on or after that date. Laws 2023, chapter 70, article 10, section 16, the effective date.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.981">
        <h1 class="shn">256.981 TRAINING OF WELFARE FRAUD PROSECUTORS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioners of human services and children, youth, and families shall, to the extent an appropriation is provided for this purpose, contract with the county attorney's council or other public or private entity experienced in providing training for prosecutors to conduct quarterly workshops and seminars focusing on current Minnesota family investment program issues, other income maintenance program changes, recovery issues, alternative sentencing methods, use of technical aids for interviews and interrogations, and other matters affecting prosecution of welfare fraud cases.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.981.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/403/#laws.2.154.0">1987 c 403 art 2 s 154</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/85/#laws.4.14.0">1997 c 85 art 4 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/159/#laws.0.48.0">1999 c 159 s 48</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.79.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 79</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.982">
        <h1 class="shn">256.982 TRAINING OF WELFARE FRAUD INVESTIGATORS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioners of human services and children, youth, and families shall, to the extent an appropriation is provided for this purpose, establish a pilot project for further education and training of welfare fraud investigators. The commissioner may enter into contractual agreements with other state, federal, or county agencies as part of cooperative projects employing experienced investigators to provide on-the-job training to county investigators.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.982.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/403/#laws.2.155.0">1987 c 403 art 2 s 155</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.80.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 80</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.983">
        <h1 class="shn">256.983 FRAUD PREVENTION INVESTIGATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.983.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Programs established.</span></h2>
          <p>Within the limits of available appropriations, the commissioners of human services and children, youth, and families shall require the maintenance of budget neutral fraud prevention investigation programs in the counties or Tribal agencies participating in the fraud prevention investigation project established under this section. If funds are sufficient, the commissioners may also extend fraud prevention investigation programs to other counties or Tribal agencies provided the expansion is budget neutral to the state. Under any expansion, the commissioners jointly have the final authority in decisions regarding the creation and realignment of individual county, Tribal agency, or regional operations. The commissioners may establish a joint office or interagency agreement to facilitate joint oversight and administration of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.981">256.981</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.9861">256.9861</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.9866">256.9866</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.983.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">County and Tribal agency proposals.</span></h2>
          <p>Each participating county and Tribal agency shall develop and submit an annual staffing and funding proposal to the commissioners no later than April 30 of each year. Each proposal shall include, but not be limited to, the staffing and funding of the fraud prevention investigation program, a job description for investigators involved in the fraud prevention investigation program, and the organizational structure of the county or Tribal agency unit, training programs for case workers, and the operational requirements which may be directed by the commissioners. The proposal shall be approved, to include any changes directed or negotiated by the commissioners, no later than June 30 of each year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.983.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Department responsibilities.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioners shall establish training programs which shall be attended by all investigative and supervisory staff of the involved county and Tribal agencies. The commissioners shall also develop the necessary operational guidelines, forms, and reporting mechanisms that shall be used by the involved county or Tribal agencies. An individual's application or redetermination form for public assistance benefits, including child care assistance programs and medical care programs, must include an authorization for release by the individual to obtain documentation for any information on that form which is involved in a fraud prevention investigation. The authorization for release is effective for six months after public assistance benefits have ceased.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.983.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Funding.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) County and Tribal agency reimbursement shall be made through the settlement provisions applicable to the Supplemental Nutrition Assistance Program (SNAP), MFIP, child care assistance programs, the medical assistance program, and other federal and state-funded programs.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioners will maintain program compliance if for any three consecutive month period, a county or Tribal agency fails to comply with fraud prevention investigation program guidelines, or fails to meet the cost-effectiveness standards developed by the commissioners. This result is contingent on the commissioners providing written notice, including an offer of technical assistance, within 30 days of the end of the third or subsequent month of noncompliance. The county or Tribal agency shall be required to submit a corrective action plan to the commissioners within 30 days of receipt of a notice of noncompliance. Failure to submit a corrective action plan or, continued deviation from standards of more than ten percent after submission of a corrective action plan, will result in denial of funding for each subsequent month, or billing the county or Tribal agency for fraud prevention investigation (FPI) service provided by the commissioners, or reallocation of program grant funds, or investigative resources, or both, to other counties or Tribal agencies. The denial of funding shall apply to the general settlement received by the county or Tribal agency on a quarterly basis and shall not reduce the grant amount applicable to the FPI project.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.983.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Child care providers; financial misconduct.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A county or Tribal agency may conduct investigations of financial misconduct by child care providers as described in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142E.50">142E.50</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142E.58">142E.58</a>. Prior to opening an investigation, a county or Tribal agency must contact the commissioners to determine whether an investigation under this chapter may compromise an ongoing investigation.</p>
          <p>(b) If, upon investigation, a preponderance of evidence shows a provider committed an intentional program violation, intentionally gave the county or Tribe materially false information on the provider's billing forms, provided false attendance records to a county, Tribe, or the commissioners, or committed financial misconduct as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142E.50#stat.142E.50.8">142E.50, subdivision 8</a>, the county or Tribal agency may recommend that the commissioners suspend a provider's payment pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142E.50">142E.50</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142E.58">142E.58</a>, or deny or revoke a provider's authorization pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142E.17#stat.142E.17.9">142E.17, subdivision 9</a>, paragraph (d), clause (2), prior to pursuing other available remedies.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.983.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.5.41.0">1989 c 282 art 5 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.5.27.0">1991 c 292 art 5 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.6.24.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 6 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/178/#laws.2.23.0">1995 c 178 art 2 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/207/#laws.2.32.0">1995 c 207 art 2 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/85/#laws.5.11.0">1997 c 85 art 5 s 11</a>,12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/159/#laws.0.49.0">1999 c 159 s 49</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/205/#laws.1.57.0">1999 c 205 art 1 s 57</a>,58; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/260/#laws.0.97.0">2000 c 260 s 97</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.1.106.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 1 s 106</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/79/#laws.2.10.0">2009 c 79 art 2 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.1.42.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 1 s 42</a>; art 2 s 110; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.1.6.0">2021 c 30 art 1 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.13.24.0">2023 c 70 art 13 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.81.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 81</a>; art 5 s 7</p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to subdivision 5 by Laws 2023, chapter 70, article 13, section 24, is effective April 28, 2025. Laws 2023, chapter 70, article 13, section 24, the effective date.</p>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.256.9831">
        <h1>256.9831</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9831.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.13">142A.13, subd 14</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9831.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.97.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 97</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9831.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.97.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 97</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.984">
        <h1 class="shn">256.984 DECLARATION AND PENALTY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.984.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Declaration.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Every application for public assistance under this chapter or chapters 142G, 256B, 256D, and 256L; child care programs under chapter 142E; and the Supplemental Nutrition Assistance Program (SNAP) benefits under chapter 393 shall be in writing or reduced to writing as prescribed by the state agency and shall contain the following declaration which shall be signed by the applicant:</p>
          <p class="bi">"I declare under the penalties of perjury that this application has been examined by me and to the best of my knowledge is a true and correct statement of every material point. I understand that a person convicted of perjury may be sentenced to imprisonment of not more than five years or to payment of a fine of not more than $10,000, or both."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.984.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>Any person who willfully and falsely makes the declaration in subdivision 1 is guilty of perjury and shall be subject to the penalties prescribed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.48">609.48</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.984.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.5.28.0">1991 c 292 art 5 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/85/#laws.5.13.0">1997 c 85 art 5 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/9/#laws.10.66.0">1Sp2001 c 9 art 10 s 66</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.1.2.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.2.20.0">2007 c 147 art 2 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.1.42.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 1 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.4.26.0">2024 c 80 art 4 s 26</a>; art 5 s 7; art 7 s 12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.42.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 42</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.256.985"><b>256.985</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.6.56.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 6 s 56</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.256.9850"><b>256.9850</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/159/#laws.0.154.0">1999 c 159 s 154</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.986">
        <h1 class="shn">256.986 COUNTY COORDINATION OF FRAUD CONTROL ACTIVITIES.</h1>
        <p>(a) The county agency shall prepare and submit to the commissioners of human services and children, youth, and families by April 30 of each state fiscal year a plan to coordinate county duties related to the prevention, investigation, and prosecution of fraud in public assistance programs. Each county must submit its first annual plan prior to April 30, 1998.</p>
        <p>(b) Within the limits of appropriations specifically made available for this purpose, the commissioners may make grants to counties submitting plans under paragraph (a) to implement coordination activities.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.986.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/178/#laws.2.25.0">1995 c 178 art 2 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/85/#laws.5.14.0">1997 c 85 art 5 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.83.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 83</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.9861">
        <h1 class="shn">256.9861 FRAUD CONTROL; PROGRAM INTEGRITY REINVESTMENT PROJECT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9861.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Program established.</span></h2>
          <p>Within the limits of available state and federal appropriations, the commissioners of human services and children, youth, and families shall make funding available to county agencies for fraud control efforts and require the maintenance of county efforts and financial contributions that were in place during fiscal year 1996.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9861.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">County proposals.</span></h2>
          <p>Each included county shall develop and submit annual funding, staffing, and operating grant proposals to the commissioners no later than April 30 of each year for the purpose of allocating federal and state funding and appropriations. Each proposal shall provide information on:</p>
          <p>(1) the staffing and funding of the fraud investigation and prosecution operations;</p>
          <p>(2) job descriptions for agency fraud control staff;</p>
          <p>(3) contracts covering outside investigative agencies;</p>
          <p>(4) operational methods to integrate the use of fraud prevention investigation techniques; and</p>
          <p>(5) implementation and utilization of administrative disqualification hearings and diversions by the existing county fraud control and prosecution procedures.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9861.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Department responsibilities.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioners shall provide written instructions outlining the contents of the proposals to be submitted under this section. Instructions shall be made available 30 days prior to the date by which proposals under subdivision 2 must be submitted. The commissioners shall establish training programs which shall be attended by fraud control staff of all involved counties. The commissioners shall also develop the necessary operational guidelines, forms, and reporting mechanisms which shall be used by the involved counties.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9861.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Standards.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioners shall, after consultation with the involved counties, establish standards governing the performance levels of county investigative units based on grant agreements with the county agencies. The standards shall take into consideration and may include investigative caseloads, grant savings levels, the comparison of fraud prevention and prosecution directed investigations, utilization levels of administrative disqualification hearings, the timely reporting and implementation of disqualifications, and the timeliness of the submission of statistical reports.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9861.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Funding.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) State funding shall be made available contingent on counties submitting a plan that is approved by the Departments of Human Services and Children, Youth, and Families. Failure or delay in obtaining that approval shall not, however, eliminate the obligation to maintain fraud control efforts at the June 30, 1996, level. County agency reimbursement shall be made through the settlement provisions applicable to the MFIP, Supplemental Nutrition Assistance Program (SNAP), and medical assistance program.</p>
          <p>(b) Should a county agency fail to comply with the standards set, or fail to meet cost-effectiveness standards developed by the commissioners for any three-month period, the commissioners shall deny reimbursement or administrative costs, after allowing an opportunity to establish compliance.</p>
          <p>(c) Any denial of reimbursement under paragraph (b) is contingent on the commissioners providing written notice, including an offer of technical assistance, within 30 days of the end of the third or subsequent months of noncompliance. The county agency shall be required to submit a corrective action plan to the commissioners within 30 days of receipt of a notice of noncompliance. Failure to submit a corrective action plan or continued deviation from standards of more than ten percent after submission of corrective action plan, will result in denial of funding for each such month during the grant year, or billing of the county agency for program integrity reinvestment project services provided by the commissioners or reallocation of grant funds to other counties. The denial of funding shall apply to the general settlement received by the county agency on a quarterly basis and shall not reduce the grant amount applicable to the program integrity reinvestment project.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.9861.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/207/#laws.2.33.0">1995 c 207 art 2 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/85/#laws.5.15.0">1997 c 85 art 5 s 15</a>-18; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/159/#laws.0.50.0">1999 c 159 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.1.106.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 1 s 106</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.1.42.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 1 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.84.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 84</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.256.9862">
        <h1>256.9862</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9862.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.13">142A.13, subd 10</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9862.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.13">142A.13, subd 11</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.256.9863"><b>256.9863</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.13">142A.13, subd 5</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.9864">
        <h1 class="shn">256.9864 REPORTS BY RECIPIENT.</h1>
        <p>(a) An assistance unit with a recent work history or with earned income shall report monthly to the county agency on income received and other circumstances affecting eligibility or assistance amounts. All other assistance units shall report on income and other circumstances affecting eligibility and assistance amounts, as specified by the state agency.</p>
        <p>(b) An assistance unit required to submit a report on the form designated by the commissioner and within ten days of the due date or the date of the significant change, whichever is later, or otherwise report significant changes which would affect eligibility or assistance amounts, is considered to have continued its application for assistance effective the date the required report is received by the county agency, if a complete report is received within a calendar month in which assistance was received.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.9864.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/85/#laws.5.20.0">1997 c 85 art 5 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/407/#laws.6.11.0">1998 c 407 art 6 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>This section is repealed by Laws 2023, chapter 70, article 10, section 98, effective March 1, 2025. Laws 2023, chapter 70, article 10, section 98, the effective date.</p>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.256.9865">
        <h1>256.9865</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9865.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.13">142A.13, subd 6</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9865.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.13">142A.13, subd 7</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9865.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.13">142A.13, subd 8</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.9865.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.13">142A.13, subd 9</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.9866">
        <h1 class="shn">256.9866 COMMUNITY SERVICE AS A COUNTY OBLIGATION.</h1>
        <p>Community service shall be an acceptable sentencing option but shall not reduce the state or federal share of any amount to be repaid or any subsequent recovery. Any reduction or offset of any such amount ordered by a court shall be treated as follows:</p>
        <p>(1) any reduction in an overpayment amount, to include the amount ordered as restitution, shall not reduce the underlying amount established as an overpayment by the state or county agency;</p>
        <p>(2) total overpayments shall continue as a debt owed and may be recovered by any civil or administrative means otherwise available to the state or county agency; and</p>
        <p>(3) any amount ordered to be offset against any overpayment shall be deducted from the county share only of any recovery and shall be based on the prevailing state minimum wage.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.9866.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/85/#laws.5.22.0">1997 c 85 art 5 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/277/#laws.0.8.0">2002 c 277 s 8</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.987">
        <h1 class="shn">256.987 ELECTRONIC BENEFIT TRANSFER CARD.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.987.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Electronic benefit transfer (EBT) card.</span></h2>
          <p>Cash benefits for the general assistance and Minnesota supplemental aid programs under chapter 256D and programs under chapter 142G must be issued on an EBT card that meets the requirements in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.13">142A.13</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.987.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.13">142A.13, subd 2</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.987.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.13">142A.13, subd 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.256.987.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.13">142A.13, subd 4</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.987.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/9/#laws.1.10.0">1Sp2011 c 9 art 1 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/247/#laws.3.6.0">2012 c 247 art 3 s 6</a>-9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.1.42.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 1 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.10.17.0">2023 c 70 art 10 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.85.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 85</a>; art 7 s 12</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.256.9871"><b>256.9871</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.13">142A.13, subd 12</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.256.9872"><b>256.9872</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.13">142A.13, subd 13</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.99">
        <h1 class="shn">256.99 REVERSE MORTGAGE PROCEEDS DISREGARDED.</h1>
        <p>All reverse mortgage loan proceeds received, including interest or earnings thereon, shall be disregarded and shall not be considered available to the borrower for purposes of determining initial or continuing eligibility for, or amount of, medical assistance, Minnesota supplemental assistance, general assistance, or a federal or state low interest loan or grant. This section applies regardless of the time elapsed since the loan was made or the disposition of the proceeds.</p>
        <p>For purposes of medical assistance eligibility provided under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.055">256B.055</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.056">256B.056</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.06">256B.06</a>, proceeds from a reverse mortgage must be disregarded as income in the month of receipt but are a resource if retained after the month of receipt.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.99.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/265/#laws.0.2.0">1979 c 265 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/3/3/#laws.0.16.0">3Sp1981 c 3 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/252/#laws.0.18.0">1985 c 252 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/689/#laws.2.268.0">1988 c 689 art 2 s 268</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/414/#laws.1.35.0">1996 c 414 art 1 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.2.71.0">2016 c 158 art 2 s 71</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.256.991">
        <h1 class="shn">256.991 RULES.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of human services may promulgate rules as necessary to implement sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.418#stat.142A.418.2">142A.418, subdivision 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.01#stat.256.01.2">256.01, subdivision 2</a>; and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/261.23">261.23</a>. The commissioner shall promulgate rules to establish standards and criteria for deciding which medical assistance services require prior authorization and for deciding whether a second medical opinion is required for an elective surgery. The commissioner shall promulgate rules as necessary to establish the methods and standards for determining inappropriate utilization of medical assistance services.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.256.991.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
